Lucas wanted American education to cultivate "emotional intelligence" and return to the methods of [[Wikipedia:Plato|Plato]] and [[Wikipedia:Shoemaking|cobblers']] apprentices before the [[Wikipedia:Industrial Revolution|Industrial Revolution]]<ref name="Rose 2004">{{YouTube|video=ANFhOdhC9Xw|text=George Lucas 2004 Interview|channel=@remembranceofthingspast6570|name=Remembrance of Things Past}}</ref>&mdash;to teach the process of how to learn rather than just feeding information<ref name="Bill Bradley" /> and to help [[Wikipedia:Evolution of the brain|human brains adapt]] to "human nature's" dichotomy between being "self-absorbed," which he compared to [[Wikipedia:Cancer|cancer]] cells, and being a "communal creature," comparable to typical cells that cooperate to keep the larger organism alive.<ref name="Boston Lucas" /> Lucas' introductory words on [[Wikipedia:George Lucas Educational Foundation|Edutopia]], the website of the George Lucas Educational Foundation, declares that "education is the foundation of our democracy."<ref name="Edutopia">{{WebCite|url=https://www.edutopia.org/about/word-george-lucas-edutopias-role-education|text=A Word from George Lucas: Edutopia's Role in Education|work=[[Wikipedia:Edutopia|Edutopia]]|archivedate=20240816183218}}</ref> After retiring from Lucasfilm, thinking that the social status of narrative art had dwindled in the [[Wikipedia:20th century|20th century]], Lucas dedicated his public efforts on the [[Wikipedia:Lucas Museum of Narrative Art|Lucas Museum of Narrative Art]], set to open in [[Wikipedia:Chicago|Chicago]] in [[2026]].<ref name="Chicago">{{OfficialYouTube|video=BDjXJqdXD-I|text=George Lucas in conversation with Charlie Rose|channel=@ChicagoTribune|name=Chicago Tribune|date=2014-10-24}}</ref>

====20th century mythology====
{{Quote|History is fiction, but people seem to think otherwise. The thing I like about fantasy and science fiction is that you can take issues, pull them out of their cultural straitjackets, and talk about them without bringing in folk artifacts that make people get closed minded. ''[&hellip;]'' By making the film "about" something other than what it's really about. Which is what mythology is, and what storytelling has always been about. Art is about communicating with people emotionally without the intellectual artifacts of the current situation, and dealing with very emotional issues.|George Lucas|<ref name="Wired" />}}
[[File:THX-1138.jpg|thumb|right|350px|Lucas' 1971 film ''THX 1138'' features a society that is tightly controlled without people's consent.]]
Born in [[1940s|1944]] and raised in [[Wikipedia:Modesto, California|Modesto, California]], USA and having grown up on [[Wikipedia:Western film|Western genre films]] and the stories of [[Wikipedia:Buck Rogers|Buck Rogers]] and [[Wikipedia:Flash Gordon|Flash Gordon]], then spending much of his youth racing cars until a near-death accident before studying anthropology and filmmaking, George Lucas' ''Star Wars'' serves, from the creator's point of view, as a modern mythology<ref name="Charlie Rose full" />&mdash;according to Lucas, mythology is narrative art that broadly and emotionally appeals to society, and it is required for people to be kept connected and for a culture's values to be passed on, rather than just individual expression for its own sake.<ref name="Bill Bradley" /> Lucas grew up in 1960s [[Wikipedia:San Francisco|San Francisco]] and has been a "very ardent [[Wikipedia:Patriotism|patriot]]" of America and a believer of democracy.<ref name="CBS 2012" /> When he started working on ''Star Wars'', he thought that America no longer had contemporary folklore for young people to enjoy.<ref name="YBY" /> Lucas asserted in an interview in 2005 that most stories have been made for adolescents as they are more open-minded than adults. In his mind, as "old stories" about "timeless" themes need to be retold to new generations in ways that resonate with them, narrative art is akin to raising children: they need to be reassured that they are part of a community who share "good" and "bad" aspects of human nature, and they need to be taught the consequences of acting on some of those notions, rather than learn everything "the hard way."<ref name="Wired" /> Speaking with journalist [[Wikipedia:Hank Stuever|Hank Stuever]] in 2015, Lucas defined art as the communication of emotions; "you get a feeling about something, you get knowledge about something, but you don't know why ''[&hellip;]'' you gotta go see it."<ref name="Stuever art">{{WebCite|url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/lifestyle/style/george-lucas-to-feel-the-true-force-of-star-wars-he-had-to-learn-to-let-it-go/2015/11/27/d752067a-8b1f-11e5-be8b-1ae2e4f50f76_story.html|text=George Lucas: To feel the true force of 'Star Wars,' he had to learn to let it go|author=[[Wikipedia:Hank Stuever|Hank Stuever]]|date=2015-12-05|work=[[Wikipedia:The Washington Post|''The Washington Post'']]|archivedate=20160101210924}}</ref> Lucas also told Nathan Heller in 2026 that film and moving images as an art is distinguished by movement, which creates emotion based on the editing of footage, as with how the same, "very neutral face" of C-3PO can be spliced with scenes indicating terror or joy.<ref name="Film and narrative art">{{WebCite|url=https://www.vogue.com/article/lucas-museum-of-narrative-art-los-angeles|text=Inside the Otherworldly Lucas Museum of Narrative Art in Los Angeles|author=Nathan Heller|date=2026-05-29|work=[[Wikipedia:Vogue (magazine)|''Vogue'']]|archivedate=20260531055115}}</ref>

Lucas wanted to explore the underpinning psychological motifs that have been told throughout human mythologies across historical societies and religions. Not only drawing from his interest in narrative literature, popular fiction, and experimental cinema but also influenced by his [[Wikipedia:Methodism|Christian Methodist]] upbringing and [[Wikipedia:Buddhism|Buddhist]] ideas, Lucas was additionally informed by the writings of anthropologist [[Wikipedia:Carlos Castaneda|Carlos Castaneda]] and [[Wikipedia:Comparative mythology|comparative mythologist]] [[Wikipedia:Joseph Campbell|Joseph Campbell]] in making ''Star Wars'' as an exploration of what he consistently throughout the decades termed "psychological archaeology."<ref name="Charlie Rose full">{{YouTube|video=6jWtbJxzGpQ|text=George Lucas Calls Disney "White Slavers" in Charlie Rose interview|channel=LaurentTouilTartour|name=Laurent Touil-Tartour}}</ref><ref name="OT Archives">''[[The Star Wars Archives: Episodes IV–VI, 1977–1983]]''</ref> Lucas became friends with Campbell in 1984, inviting him to Skywalker Ranch to give talks, which informed ''Star Wars'' composer [[John Williams]] among many others about the crucial role of mythologies such as ''Star Wars'' in the development of societies from the [[Wikipedia:Neolithic Revolution|Neolithic Revolution]] to the [[Wikipedia:Space Age|Space Age]]. The ranch also hosted journalist [[Wikipedia:Bill Moyers|Bill Moyers']] [[1988]] documentary television series [[Wikipedia:The Power of Myth|''The Power of Myth'']] with Campbell and another TV interview with Lucas about the mythology of ''Star Wars'' in 1999, exploring the role of storytelling and motifs of the mythological "[[Wikipedia:Hero's journey|hero's journey]]."<ref name="Mythic Discovery I">{{SW|url=news/mythic-discovery-within-the-inner-reaches-of-outer-space-joseph-campbell-meets-george-lucas-part-i|text=Mythic Discovery Within the Inner Reaches of Outer Space: Joseph Campbell Meets George Lucas - Part I}}</ref>

[[File:Luc0-035.jpg|thumb|left|260px|Francis Ford Coppola, R2-D2, and George Lucas]]
Lucas said that ''Star Wars'' belonged to the "heroic myth" tradition.<ref name="Bill Bradley" /> Originally, Lucas and his filmmaker friend [[Wikipedia:Francis Ford Coppola|Francis Ford Coppola]] were collaborating on [[Wikipedia:Apocalypse Now|''Apocalypse Now'']], a film about American democracy in the context of the country's then-ongoing [[Wikipedia:Vietnam War|war]] in [[Wikipedia:Vietnam|Vietnam]]. ''Apocalypse Now'' was released in [[1979]] under the direction of Coppola, who fashioned the film after the serious manner of [[Wikipedia:Joseph Conrad|Joseph Conrad's]] 1899 novella [[Wikipedia:Heart of Darkness|''Heart of Darkness'']], deviating from Lucas' wish to produce a satire akin to [[Wikipedia:Stanley Kubrick|Stanley Kubrick's]] 1964 film [[Wikipedia:Dr. Strangelove|''Dr. Strangelove or: How I Learned to Stop Worrying and Love the Bomb'']].<ref name="Cannes 2024" /> Lucas thus focused on his personal [[Wikipedia:Fantasy|fantasy]] project, writing a [[The Star Wars rough draft|rough draft]] of ''Star Wars'' in [[1974]] following the success of his [[1973]] film [[Wikipedia:American Graffiti|''American Graffiti'']]. His draft describes the Empire as "America ten years from now," at a "turning point" of "fascism or revolution." "Nixonian gangsters"&mdash;referring to [[Wikipedia:Richard Nixon|Richard Nixon]], [[Wikipedia:President of the United States|President of the United States]] from 1969 to August 1974&mdash;would have assassinated the Emperor and rigged an election to take power, starting "race riots" and helping rebel groups before imposing a "[[Wikipedia:Totalitarianism|'total control']] [[Wikipedia:Police state|police state]]," firstly welcomed by the people who are frustrated with rampant criminality, then exploiting them with high taxes and utility and transport costs. Lucas's first theatrical film, [[1971]]'s [[Wikipedia:THX 1138|''THX 1138'']], is set in a dystopian consumerist society tightly controlled by robotic police.<ref name="Making">''[[The Making of Star Wars: The Definitive Story Behind the Original Film]]''</ref> His earlier short films included abstract tone poems, a [[Wikipedia:Look at Life (film)|montage]] on American race riots,<ref name="AAA">{{OfficialYouTube|video=djBSxW4PrmY|text=George Lucas, Academy Class of 1989, Full Interview|channel=@AcademyofAchievementOrg|name=Academy of Achievement|date=2016-07-23}} &mdash; Includes an interview dated [[June 3]], [[1995]], and an interview dated [[June 19]], [[1999]]</ref> and a [[Wikipedia:Freiheit (film)|narrative film]] on freedom set at the [[Wikipedia:Berlin Wall|Berlin Wall]] of [[Wikipedia:East Germany|East Germany]].<ref name="DW">{{OfficialYouTube|video=VvIdxnTULJc|text=Star Wars Director George Lucas in his own words|channel=DWHistoryandCulture|name=DW History and Culture|date=2024-02-04}}</ref> Lucas had a documentary filmmaking background and preferred its spontaneous approach and experimentations with the medium.<ref name="Corliss">{{WebCite|url=https://content.time.com/time/arts/article/0,8599,1173216,00.html|text=A Conversation with George Lucas|author=[[Wikipedia:Richard Corliss|Richard Corliss]]|date=2006-03-14|work=[[Wikipedia:Time (magazine)|TIME]]|archivedate=20140604071149}}</ref>

====Changing reality====
{{Quote|<nowiki />''[&hellip;]'' while the psychological basis of ''Star Wars'' is mythological, the political and social bases are historical. I like to take things and strip them down, then use the model and build a different story on it. ''[&hellip;]'' I was sitting around with a dozen reporters, and the Russian correspondents all thought the film was about Russian politics, and the Americans all thought it was about Bush. And I said, "Well, it's really based on Rome. And on the French Revolution and Bonaparte." It's shocking that these things get repeated through history. The same mistakes get made and the tension between democracy and tyranny is always the same. And we haven't figured out any way around it.|George Lucas, in 2005|<ref name="Boston Lucas" />}}
Lucas realized after ''THX''{{'s}} release that "being pessimistic doesn't seem to accomplish anything"<ref name="YBY">''[[Star Wars Year By Year: A Visual History, New Edition]]''</ref> and instead wanted to make a light-hearted film that would appeal to the public<ref name="Charlie Rose full" /> and "regenerate optimism" for young people who were apathetic to how America was "being ruined."<ref name="TROS Vanity">{{WebCite|url=https://www.vanityfair.com/hollywood/2019/05/star-wars-cover-story|text=Star Wars: The Rise of Skywalker Exclusive, Behind-the-Scenes Cover Story|author=Lev Grossman|date=2019-05-22|work=[[Wikipedia:Vanity Fair (magazine)|''Vanity Fair'']]|archivedate=20260201060346}}</ref> In particular, the filmmaker wished to make a "[[Wikipedia:Fairy tale|fairytale]]" for "twelve-year-olds" given the "dark" times of the Vietnam War and the American [[Wikipedia:Civil rights movement|civil rights movement]].<ref name="Cannes 2024" /> He read [[Wikipedia:John Carter of Mars (collection)|''John Carter of Mars'']] by [[Wikipedia:Edgar Rice Burroughs|Edgar Rice Burroughs]] and [[Wikipedia:John Coleman Burroughs|John Coleman Burroughs]], [[Wikipedia:James George Frazer|James George Frazer's]] [[Wikipedia:The Golden Bough|''The Golden Bough: A Study in Magic and Religion'']], and fantasy books for inspiration in January 1974. Lucas was also influenced by a host of films in writing ''Star Wars'', including [[Wikipedia:Akira Kurosawa|Akira Kurosawa's]] Japanese period dramas and [[Wikipedia:Fritz Lang|Fritz Lang's]] [[Wikipedia:Metropolis (1927 film)|''Metropolis'']] (1927), [[Wikipedia:Michael Curtiz|Michael Curtiz's]] [[Wikipedia:The Adventures of Robin Hood|''The Adventures of Robin Hood'']] (1938), and [[Wikipedia:Howard Hawks|Howard Hawks']] [[Wikipedia:Air Force (film)|''Air Force'']] (1943).<ref name="YBY" />

[[File:Lucas.jpg|thumb|right|200px|George Lucas]]
In 1977, Lucas stated that the Force "is a perception of the reality that exists around us,"<ref name="Making" /> and in 1999's [[Star Wars: Episode I The Phantom Menace|''Star Wars'': Episode I ''The Phantom Menace'']], Qui-Gon Jinn advised Anakin Skywalker that "your focus determines your reality."<ref name="TPM" /> He wanted to make a feature film with a bigger budget that involved soundstages, art directors, production designers and fulfilled the mythologizing role of American Westerns that he had grown up watching.<ref name="Charlie Rose full" /> Lucas remarked in March 1999 that "one of the weird and more interesting things about ''Star Wars''" was that it has been well-received across "many cultures" worldwide. Despite low expectations during the making of the first film, it was a major financial success while Lucas also retained creative and marketing freedoms with distributor [[20th Century Studios|20th Century Fox]]. The entrepreneur fully owned and directed his own franchise company, [[Lucasfilm]]. He criticized corporations and especially the media of the United States for answering to nothing but the urge to make more money, which has in his view the effect of making people "disgusted" with their own culture and government and become cynical with "everything."<ref name="Cynical optimism" /> Speaking about a [[Wikipedia:French people|Frenchman]] he had met who claimed to watch ''Star Wars'' every week in [[1997]], [[LucasArts]] president [[Jack Sorensen]] commented: "I don't know if I want to say this in print, but I feel like ''Star Wars'' is the mythology of a nonsectarian world. It describes how people want to live. People all view politics as corrupt, much more so in Europe than here ''[the United States]'', and yet people are not cynical underneath&mdash;they want to believe in something pure, noble. That's ''Star Wars''." Sorensen also thought it striking that the franchise's literary elements, while seemingly "uncommercial" and "subordinate to the purely cinematic elements" when Lucas was making his films, had become "the very qualities that seem commercial about it now."<ref name="Force still with us">{{WebCite|url=https://www.newyorker.com/magazine/1997/01/06/why-is-the-force-still-with-us-star-wars-george-lucas|text=Why Is the Force Still with Us?|author=John Seabrook|date=1997-12-30|work=[[Wikipedia:The New Yorker|''The New Yorker'']]|archivedate=20260204154832}}</ref>

[[File:SkywalkerRanchMainHouse.jpg|thumb|left|350px|Skywalker Ranch, Lucas' studio]]
US President [[Wikipedia:Jimmy Carter|Jimmy Carter]] watched ''Star Wars'' alongside [[Wikipedia:Rosalynn Carter|Rosalynn Carter]] and [[Wikipedia:President of Egypt|Egyptian President]] [[Wikipedia:Anwar Sadat|Anwar Sadat]] and [[Wikipedia:Jehan Sadat|Jehan Sadat]] on [[February 4]], [[1978]], at [[Wikipedia:Camp David|Camp David]],<ref name="Camp David">{{WebCite|url=https://www.jimmycarterlibrary.gov/sites/default/files/pdf_documents/assets/documents/diary/1978/d020478t.pdf|text=February 4, 1978|work=The daily diary of President Jimmy Carter|archivedate=20241114155740}}</ref> where [[Wikipedia:Camp David Accords|peace accords]] between [[Wikipedia:Egypt|Egypt]] and [[Wikipedia:Israel|Israel]] were eventually signed in September. George Lucas, unhappy with writing and also stressed by directing, later hired [[Irvin Kershner]] to direct [[Star Wars: Episode V The Empire Strikes Back|''Star Wars'': Episode V ''The Empire Strikes Back'']] as he was impressed with Kershner's quick turnaround on the 1977 film [[Wikipedia:Raid on Entebbe (film)|''Raid on Entebbe'']] about Israel's [[Wikipedia:Entebbe raid|operation]] in [[Wikipedia:Uganda|Uganda]] in the preceding year, and the ''Star Wars'' sequel was released in May [[1980]].<ref name="YBY" /> Lucas remarked in a conversation televised in [[2002]] with [[Carrie Fisher]], who played Princess Leia Organa, that the "hero's journey" of Anakin Skywalker would involve him becoming "a bad person" through "very good intentions"&mdash;they alluded to a common opinion in the US at the time supporting "vengeance" for the [[Wikipedia:September 11 attacks|September 11 terrorist attacks]] of [[2001]] that sparked the "[[Wikipedia:War on terror|war on terror]]" and how Lucas had originally titled [[1983]]'s [[Star Wars: Episode VI Return of the Jedi|Episode VI]] "''Revenge of the Jedi''" rather than "''Return of the Jedi''."<ref name="Fisher" /> In 2010, George Lucas was invited to the launch of the UK charity Films Without Borders, which brought teenage filmmakers from Israel, [[Wikipedia:Palestine|Palestine]], and [[Wikipedia:Rwanda|Rwanda]] together, and advised the students that humans have always been telling the same stories for millennia but differ in the way they tell the stories, and good filmmakers entertain audiences in return for their time and money instead of directly preaching messages&mdash;"themes are always best unsaid," and the process of entertaining would then "hopefully" give insight into the audience's own lives and create dialogue across troubled divides.<ref name="Films Without Borders">{{WebCite|url=https://www.thejc.com/life/film/george-lucas-star-wars-peace-plan-e8nzhyau|text=George Lucas' Star Wars peace plan|author=Jessica Elgot|date=2010-04-29|work=[[Wikipedia:The Jewish Chronicle|''The Jewish Chronicle'']]|archivedate=20250327231745}}</ref>

===Echoes of history===
====Empire of liberty: a new republic, repressing Native Americans, and Nixon====
{{Quote|And here's an interesting thing about democracy: if you don't treat it well, if you don't do your job, especially if you're our representative in the senate, in the parliament, or whatever, the whole thing can go awry, because if you're always bickering and not agreeing on things and doing the people's work&mdash;who elected you&mdash;a tyrant will come in and take over and do it for you because the people want to get the job done and they will tend to go where the person who's got the most leadership is.|George Lucas|<ref name="Cannes 2005">{{YouTube|video=4rmnO46iKrw|text=George Lucas' interview about politics, democracy and dictartorship|channel=@swsagafan8779|name=SW Saga Fan}} &mdash; excerpt from a [[Wikipedia:BBC|BBC Newsnight]] report at the [[Wikipedia:Cannes Film Festival|Cannes Film Festival]], [[2005]]</ref>}}
[[File:Princessleiaheadwithgun.jpg|thumb|right|250px|Carrie Fisher as Leia, rebel leader and princess of the people]]
According to a [[2024]] interview with the broadcaster [[Wikipedia:Deutsche Welle|Deutsche Welle]], George Lucas intended ''Star Wars'' to be one movie about the adventures of Luke Skywalker and the tragedy of Anakin Skywalker. What became the prequel trilogy originated as a backstory that was constrained by the visual effects technology of the 1970s, and the middle of the ''Star Wars'' story evolved into three films.<ref name="DW" /> Lucas told the [[Wikipedia:Time (magazine)|''TIME'']] magazine in 2002 that this original trilogy is the story of "Princess Leia and her friends" waking up to the fact that the Republic was the Empire, a "sham" democracy that they must resist.<ref name="Dark Victory">{{WebCite|url=https://content.time.com/time/subscriber/article/0,33009,1002323-4,00.html|text=Dark Victory|author=Richard Corliss and Jess Cagle|date=2002-04-29|work=[[Wikipedia:Time (magazine)|''TIME'']]|archivedate=20231207004714}}</ref> On Han Solo's initial dismissal of the revolution and acting as a mercenary and smuggler, Lucas said in August 1977 that he was just "a free-enterprise small businessman who is trading between systems without benefit of a proper license from the trade unions." The issue of smuggling "was really the result of the Empire, which has such stringent rules about tariffs, and the trade companies, which are so strong, so monopolistic. Most legitimate operators were squeezed out of the business." However, Han Solo did not consider the situation from a political perspective despite the Empire being the "[[Wikipedia:Big Brother (Nineteen Eighty-Four)|Big Brother]]," instead treating the regime as any another obstacle.<ref name="Making" />

The [[Star Wars: A New Hope (novelization)|original novelization of ''Star Wars'']] published in [[1976]] included an excerpt from the "First Saga" of the "[[Journal of the Whills/Legends|Journal of the Whills]]" that outlined the fall of the Republic with the "wise rule of the Senate and the protection of the Jedi Knights," using the parable of a tree that could "withstand any external attack" but "rotted from within;" with the help of "massive organs of commerce," Senator Palpatine was elected "President of the Republic" with the promise to reunite discontent people and "restore the remembered glory of the Republic," but he "was controlled by the very assistants and boot-lickers he had appointed to high office," with "governors and bureaucrats" overseeing a "reign of terror" using "the Imperial forces and the name of the increasingly isolated Emperor." The excerpt ends introducing a group of rebels wishing to restore the Old Republic and "cast the light of new truth" across an oppressed galaxy.<ref name="ANH novel">[[Star Wars: A New Hope (novelization)|''Star Wars: A New Hope'' novelization]]</ref> In early drafts of his film script, Lucas intended ''Star Wars'' to be akin to a saga documented by an observer known as [[Ancient Order of the Whills|the Whills]], and the plot device later developed into the Force.<ref name="Annotated">''[[Star Wars: The Annotated Screenplays]]''</ref>

Lucas described similar developments when speaking with [[Carol Titelman]] in [[August 5]], 1977, on the history of the ''Star Wars'' galaxy as general notes for Lucasfilm: the Republican Senate had single four-year terms for its Chancellors, but senators was corrupted by intergalactic companies to extend the last Chancellor's term twice due to a crisis and made him Emperor for life; senators were concerned by bloated bureaucracy and rationalized that executive powers could control it, but the Emperor made Senate meetings shorter and less frequent and had actually made the bureaucracy "run amok" in the first place, having control of key bureaucrats through company money. Although "a little over half" of the Senate was not corrupted and tried to oust the Emperor through legal means such as impeachment, many of them mysteriously died, prompting the Jedi Knights to rally for the remaining senators. However, the Jedi could not restore order as they were betrayed and destroyed by Darth Vader.<ref name="Making" /> When Lucas met [[Richard Marquand]], [[Howard G. Kazanjian|Howard Kazanjian]], and [[Lawrence Kasdan]] for a story conference in [[1981]], he explained the tragedy of Darth Vader, and that because the Emperor had been elected, "nobody knew" that he was a bad person and "Richard M. Nixon" was his name.<ref name="Archives" />

[[File:EwokRevolution.png|thumb|left|350px|Lucas compared the Ewoks that defeated the Empire in 1983's ''Return of the Jedi'' to the Vietnamese who defeated the "American Empire."]]
Speaking with Hollywood director [[Wikipedia:James Cameron|James Cameron]] in 2012, Lucas also compared the rebels and Ewoks' battle against the technologically advanced Imperial forces in ''Return of the Jedi'' with two cases of the "underdogs" against the "empire"&mdash;firstly, the "[[Wikipedia:Viet Cong|Viet Cong]]" that defeated American forces in the Vietnam War, and secondly, the founders of the United States that [[Wikipedia:American Revolution|defeated]] the "[[Wikipedia:British Empire|English Empire]]" in America in the late [[Wikipedia:18th century|18th century]].<ref name="Cameron">{{YouTube|video=Nxl3IoHKQ8c|text=JAMES CAMERON'S STORY OF SCIENCE FICTION|channel=@BehindTheScenesFilmIsnow|name=FilmIsNow Movie Bloopers & Extras}}</ref> Appearing in court in [[Wikipedia:Calgary|Calgary]], [[Wikipedia:Canada|Canada]] on a copyright claim about the concept of the Ewoks in [[1990]], Lucas testified that he was also informed by<ref name="Court">{{WebCite|url=https://www.latimes.com/archives/la-xpm-1990-01-19-ca-189-story.html|text=Lucas Testifies in Calgary Court That 'Jedi' Ewoks Were His Idea|author=Monica Zurowski|date=1990-01-19|work=[[Wikipedia:Los Angeles Times|''Los Angeles Times'']]|archivedate=20190415042706}}</ref> the [[Wikipedia:Coast Miwok|Coast Miwok]] people indigenous to [[Wikipedia:Marin County, California|Marin County, California]], whose population inhabited the land around [[Skywalker Ranch]] until it was devestated by [[Wikipedia:Native American disease and epidemics|disease]] and [[Wikipedia:California genocide|displacement]]<ref name="Stewards">{{InsiderCite|212|Stewards of the Forest Moon}}</ref> brought by European colonists, like other [[Wikipedia:Indigenous peoples of the Americas|Native American]] societies.<ref name="America">{{BookCite|author=[[Wikipedia:James W. Loewen|Loewen, James W.]]|title=[[Wikipedia:Lies My Teacher Told Me|''Lies My Teacher Told Me'']]|publisher=Touchstone|date=2007}}</ref>

====Road from Rome: Caesar, Napoleon, and Hitler====
{{Quote|The whole point of the movies, the underlying element that makes the movies work, is that you, whether you go backwards or forwards, you start out in a democracy, and democracy turns into a dictatorship, and then the rebels make it back into a democracy. That's one of the uber-issues that everyone is dealing with. You've got the personal issue of Anakin and his turn to the dark side, and his children bringing him back to being a human being, but the larger issue is the fact that you've given up your democracy. ''[&hellip;]'' It came out of Nixon wanting to change the rules so that he could get a third term, and with that&mdash;I'm a big history buff&mdash;I was beginning to study Caesar. Why did the Roman senate give his nephew a dictatorship when they had gotten rid of Caesar? Why did they do that? After they had a revolution in France to get freedom, why did they create an emperor? I mean, why did they do that? Why did the Germans, after they had a democracy after World War I, turn it into a dictatorship? Why did they do that? That was my initial question 30 years ago. And I'm not really saying why as much as how it gets done, because it always gets done in the same way. And it's scary, the fact that the world has caught up to that theory.|George Lucas, in 2005|<ref name="SciFi">{{WebCite|url=http://www.scifi.com/sfw/issue421/interview.html|text=George Lucas lets Ian McDiarmid and Hayden Christensen get their ''Revenge'' in ''Episode III''|author=Debbie Dykstra|work=[[Wikipedia:Syfy|SciFi.com]]|archivedate=20060909135351|nolive=1}}</ref>}}
[[File:McDiarmidLucasProclamation.png|thumb|right|350px|Ian McDiarmid, who likened Palpatine to Slobodan Milošević, and George Lucas, who based the character on Augustus, Napoleon, and Hitler out of concern for American democracy under Nixon.]]
1999's ''The Phantom Menace'' launched the prequel trilogy, where Lucas intended to explore how democracies could be eroded and transition into dictatorships through democratic means rather than via coups or rebellions in the real world, paralleled with the story of a good person such as Anakin Skywalker turning into a villain&mdash;Darth Vader.<ref name="Archives" /> Upon the release of 2002's [[Star Wars: Episode II Attack of the Clones|''Star Wars'': Episode II ''Attack of the Clones'']], ''TIME'' magazine writers asked Lucas about politics in ''Star Wars'', considering Obi-Wan Kenobi's cynicism of political campaign financing to be an echo of US Senator [[Wikipedia:John McCain|John McCain's]] role in [[Wikipedia:Bipartisan Campaign Reform Act|campaign financing reform]]; Padmé Amidala's idealistic plead in a deleted scene in the Senate calling the Military Creation Bill "a declaration of war," with violence begetting more violence and resulting in everyone losing their freedom an echo of [[Wikipedia:Secretary-General of the United Nations|United Nations Secretary-General]] [[Wikipedia:Kofi Annan|Kofi Annan's]] pleads for the plight of the [[Wikipedia:Palestinians|people]] of Palestine; Anakin Skywalker's [[Wikipedia:Realpolitik|realpolitik]] about a strong leader resolving the Senate's differences an echo of Roman Senator [[Wikipedia:Marcus Junius Brutus|Brutus]] in his [[Wikipedia:Assassination of Julius Caesar|assassination]] of Julius Caesar. Lucas replied that he described himself a believer in liberal ideals of democracy and suggested a continuity between his films by emphasizing the role of Princess Leia and the Jedi in the original trilogy to overthrow the Emperor.<ref name="Dark Victory" /> In 2005, actor [[Ian McDiarmid]] described the character of Palpatine/Darth Sidious as "totally irredeemable," motivated only in accumulating and exercise power for its own sake unlike the psychological complexity of Shakespeare's characters; McDiarmid instead likened Palpatine to the [[Wikipedia:Serbia and Montenegro|Serbian]] leader [[Wikipedia:Slobodan Milošević|Slobodan Milošević]], who was on trial for genocide, crimes against humanity, and war crimes in the [[Wikipedia:Yugoslav Wars|former Yugoslavia]].<ref name="SciFi" />

[[File:Triumph_of_the_Will_-_Congress_Hall.jpg|thumb|left|300px|The 1935 Nazi film ''Triumph of the Will''. Lucas worried for America's democracy, drawing on historical examples of dictators being voted into power when making ''Star Wars''.]]
Although American politics during the Vietnam War served as a catalyst for writing ''Star Wars'', Lucas cited three examples of what he believed to be the cyclical nature of history with respect to Palpatine's rise to power: firstly, Julius Caesar in the [[Wikipedia:Roman Republic|Roman Republic]]<ref name="Archives" />&mdash;Caesar was voted "[[Wikipedia:Dictator perpetuo|dictator perpetuo]]" and then usurped by fellow senators in [[Wikipedia:44 BC|44 BC]], leading to the rise of [[Wikipedia:Augustus|Augustus]] and his [[Wikipedia:Roman Empire|Roman Empire]], which dominated [[Wikipedia:Europe|Europe]], [[Wikipedia:North Africa|north Africa]], and [[Wikipedia:West Asia|west Asia]] for centuries.<ref name="Roman">{{BookCite|author=[[Wikipedia:Edward Gibbon|Gibbon, Edward]]|title=[[Wikipedia:The History of the Decline and Fall of the Roman Empire|''The History of the Decline and Fall of the Roman Empire'']]|publisher=Strahan & Cadell|date=1776&ndash;1788}}</ref> Secondly, Napoleon Bonaparte in [[Wikipedia:French Revolution|revolutionary France]]<ref name="Archives" />&mdash;following the [[Wikipedia:Declaration of the Rights of Man and of the Citizen|collapse]] of the [[Wikipedia:Ancien régime|ancien régime]] in [[Wikipedia:1789|1789]], legislators allowed his [[Wikipedia:Coup of 18 Brumaire|coup]] as "[[Wikipedia:French Consulate|Premier consul]]" in [[Wikipedia:1799|1799]] and then [[Wikipedia:Coronation of Napoleon|appoint]] himself "[[Wikipedia:Emperor of the French|Emperor]]," with [[Wikipedia:Bonapartism|Bonapartist]] [[Wikipedia:First French Empire|France]] dominating [[Wikipedia:19th century|19th century]] Europe.<ref name="Revolution">{{BookCite|author=[[Wikipedia:Eric Hobsbawm|Hobsbawm, Eric]]|title=[[Wikipedia:The Age of Revolution: Europe 1789–1848|''The Age of Revolution: Europe 1789–1848'']]|publisher=Weidenfeld & Nicolson|date=1962}}</ref> Thirdly, Adolf Hitler in the [[Wikipedia:Germany|German]] [[Wikipedia:Weimar Republic|Weimar Republic]]<ref name="Archives" />&mdash;the [[Wikipedia:German Empire|German Empire]] [[Wikipedia:German revolution of 1918–1919|collapsed]] in [[Wikipedia:1918|1918]] following [[Wikipedia:World War I|World War I]] and the Republic voted Hitler's [[Wikipedia:Nazi Party|Nazi Party]] into [[Wikipedia:Reichstag (Weimar Republic)|parliament]] and [[Wikipedia:Hitler cabinet|government]] in 1933, with [[Wikipedia:Chancellor of Germany|Chancellor]] Hitler enacting [[Wikipedia:Enabling Act of 1933|emergency powers]] in the same year that resulted in his elevation as "[[Wikipedia:Führer|führer]]," [[Wikipedia:The Holocaust|the Holocaust]] genocide of [[Wikipedia:Jews|Jewish people]], and World War II until Nazi Germany's defeat in 1945.<ref name="Third Reich">{{BookCite|author=[[Wikipedia:William L. Shirer|Shirer, William L.]]|title=[[Wikipedia:The Rise and Fall of the Third Reich|''The Rise and Fall of the Third Reich'']]|publisher=Simon & Schuster|date=1960}}</ref>

====Charisma and tyranny====
In another ''Attack of the Clones'' deleted scene, Count Dooku tells a captive Padmé Amidala that "the democratic process in the Republic is a sham" and the "incompetent" Chancellor had allowed the bureaucracy to become stronger and the Republic's "cult of greed" would eventually lose "any pretext of democracy and freedom." Amidala re-affirms her dedication to the Republic and calls Dooku's Separatists "not government that has been bought out by business" but "business becoming government!"<ref name="Archives" /> The film's [[Star Wars: Episode II Attack of the Clones (novelization)|novelization]], written by [[R. A. Salvatore]] based a script of Lucas', even includes Dooku telling the business leaders joining the Separatists that their alliance has an "absolute commitment to capitalism" regarding lowering taxation and tariffs, promising "complete free trade" by eventually abolishing "all trade barriers."<ref name="AOTC novel">[[Star Wars: Episode II Attack of the Clones (novelization)|''Star Wars'': Episode II ''Attack of the Clones'' novelization]]</ref> Lucas decided to replace the scene with Dooku speaking to a captive Obi-Wan Kenobi instead, showing the irony of Dooku sharing most of the Jedi's view that the Senate is corrupt and ineffective and also telling the truth about the Republic being controlled by Darth Sidious&mdash;yet Kenobi does not believe Dooku because the fallen Jedi asks him to join him in overthrowing Sidious and taking power by themselves.<ref name="AOTC audio">[[Star Wars: Episode II Attack of the Clones|''Star Wars'': Episode II ''Attack of the Clones'']] DVD audio commentary</ref>

[[File:Lee_Lucas_EP2_BTS.png|thumb|right|300px|George Lucas and Sir [[Christopher Lee]]]]
Tony Keen wrote in 2012's ''[[Star Wars and History]]'' about the saga's connections with the fall of the Roman Republic and compared Padmé Amidala's rebuke of Anakin Skywalker's faith in a benevolent dictator and her belief in a democratic republic with the Roman politician [[Wikipedia:Cicero|Cicero]], who criticized Julius Caesar's dictatorship, supported Caesar's heir Octavian against [[Wikipedia:Mark Antony|Mark Antony]], and was condemned to death by Antony. The latter and [[Wikipedia:Cleopatra|Cleopatra]], [[Wikipedia:Ptolemaic Kingdom|Queen of Egypt]], [[Wikipedia:War of Actium|warred]] against Octavian as lovers until their deaths, allowing Octavian to rule as the first Roman emperor as Augustus. Although contemporaneous Roman records did not distinguish the "Republic" from "Empire," nor did contemporaneous records name the "Weimar Republic" as a separate polity when Nazis came to power in Germany, Keen highlighted that Hitler used the [[Wikipedia:Reichstag fire|Reichstag fire]] as pretext for suspending civil liberties while Napoleon Bonaparte organized a formal coronation of himself, similar to Palpatine's claim of a "Jedi plot" and declaration of the Galactic Empire.<ref name="History" />

Writing in the same book, Kevin S. Decker compared the [[Wikipedia:Charismatic authority|charisma]]&mdash;and thus capacity to gain and maintain popular support as an apparent "political idealist"&mdash; of Count Dooku, whose Sith name was Darth Tyranus, to the ancient tyrants [[Wikipedia:Cypselus|Kypselus]] of [[Wikipedia:Ancient Corinth|Corinth]] and [[Wikipedia:Pisistratus|Pisistratus]] of [[Wikipedia:Athens|Athens]] in the [[Wikipedia:7th century BC|7th]] and [[Wikipedia:6th century BC|6th centuries BC]], who both seized power by appealing to marginalized people; "[[Wikipedia:Demagogue|demagogues]]," according to Aristotle. Decker further compared the Galactic Republic's first Chancellor, [[Tarsus Valorum]]<ref name="History">''[[Star Wars and History]]''</ref>&mdash;who originated in [[Star Wars Legends|''Star Wars'' Legends]] material<ref name="Bane">''[[Darth Bane: Rule of Two]]''</ref> but has since been introduced to the current [[canon]]<ref name="Tarkin" />&mdash;to [[Wikipedia:Solon|Solon]], who laid the foundation of democracy by breaking down ancient divisions and [[Wikipedia:Solonian constitution|developing]] a rule of law. The author noted that in contrast, the fall of Roman republican practices and also the Roman Empire, which had adopted [[Wikipedia:Christianity|Christianity]] given the enduring appeal of [[Wikipedia:Jesus|Jesus]], left many European monarchies that sought Christian-based [[Wikipedia:Legitimacy (political)|legitimacy]] and power deals among their [[Wikipedia:Royal court|royal courts]].<ref name="History" />

[[File:Peter_Cushing_George_Lucas_Carrie_Fisher_filming_Star_Wars.jpg|thumb|left|300px|[[Peter Cushing]], George Lucas, and Carrie Fisher]]
Within this medieval context, Decker stated that the [[Code of the Sith|Sith Code]]<ref name="History" />&mdash;written by [[David Gaider]] as a Nazi-inspired perversion of the [[Jedi Code/Legends|Jedi Code]] from Legends material,<ref name="Gaider">{{Tumblr|subdomain=the-gaider-archives|url=post/108932517363/on-knights-of-the-old-republic|name=DGaider Tumblr Archives|text=On Knights of the Old Republic}}</ref> and both codes have since introduced been in the current canon<ref name="Disciple" /><ref name="GOTW">[[Guardians of the Whills (novel)|''Guardians of the Whills'']]</ref>&mdash;would instruct adherents to be a "manipulative [[Wikipedia:Courtier|courtier]]" of a monarch, yet Darth Sidious and Tarkin go beyond this framework as the [[Wikipedia:Italian Renaissance|Italian Renaissance]] author [[Wikipedia:Niccolò Machiavelli|Niccolò Machiavelli]] advised, obtaining power by being feared rather than loved; achieving effective means even at the cost of Christian virtues. Tarkin expressed disdain for the Jedi Code and implemented a rule of constant militarization and fear under the Empire, while Sidious used coercion and cruelty&mdash;much like [[Wikipedia:Qin Shi Huang|Qin Shi Huang]], the first of the [[Wikipedia:Emperor of China|Chinese emperors]] who ruled for over two thousand years until the [[Wikipedia:1911 Revolution|Republican Revolution]] in 1912&mdash;though the Sith Lord did not follow Machiavelli's advice to refrain from indiscriminate cruelty and ultimately fails. Decker also added that the [[Wikipedia:Great man theory|conquests of one man]] could only be enabled by the public, comparing Communist Russia and China's [[Wikipedia:Cult of personality|personality cults]] of [[Wikipedia:Joseph Stalin|Joseph Stalin]] and [[Wikipedia:Mao Zedong|Mao Zedong]] to the ''Star Wars'' galaxy's "conditions of [[Wikipedia:Decadence|cultural decadence]], political amoralism, and unrestrained, all-consuming commerce and industry" in the rise of the Empire; the moral leadership of Bail Organa, Mon Mothma, and Leia Organa follow Machiavelli's praise for Aristotle, Cicero, and [[Wikipedia:John of Salisbury|John of Salisbury's]] ideas and an ideal republic of a free contitution for [[Wikipedia:Civic virtue|citizens of virtue]] to advance the common good.<ref name="History" />

====Chinese trade and empire====
=====Revolutions of culture=====
[[File:Yoda-HongKong.jpg|thumb|right|280px|''Star Wars'' toys made by a Hong Kong company in Guangdong being packaged in Hong Kong for export to the US in 1982.]]
Mao Zedong's [[Wikipedia:Cultural Revolution|Cultural Revolution]] was a period of mass societal upheaval&mdash;and restrictions on Western imports such as ''Star Wars''&mdash;in the [[Wikipedia:China|People's Republic of China]] that began to wind down with Mao's death in 1976.<ref name="Cultural Revolution">{{WebCite|url=https://variety.com/2019/film/news/disney-tencent-star-wars-china-literature-1203375011|text=Disney and Tencent to Put Out New Chinese 'Star Wars' Story|author=Rebecca Davis|date=2019-10-18|work=[[Wikipedia:Variety (magazine)|''Variety'']]|archivedate=20191019134548}}</ref> Nevertheless, the [[Wikipedia:Chinese Communist Party|Chinese Communist Party's]] newspaper, the [[Wikipedia:People's Daily|''People's Daily'']], criticized the original ''Star Wars'' film on July 24, 1978 as an illusory fairyland that reflected Americans' dissatisfaction with reality<ref name="RFI">{{WebCite|url=https://www.rfi.fr/en/chinas-secret-plan-use-force-star-wars|text=China's secret plan to use the force of 'Star Wars'|author=[[Wikipedia:Agence France-Presse|Agence France-Presse]]|date=2016-01-20|work=[[Wikipedia:Radio France Internationale|Radio France Internationale]]|archivedate=20260202094927}}</ref> and added that the supposedly corrupting downfall of capitalist culture was exemplified by the visual effects expenses on ''Star Wars'' and [[Steven Spielberg]]'s [[Wikipedia:Close Encounters of the Third Kind|''Close Encounters of the Third Kind'']],<ref name="People's Daily review">{{WebCite|url=https://www.epochtimes.com/b5/16/1/15/n4617174.htm|text=近四十年前中共黨媒評《星戰》 引網絡嘩然|date=2016-01-15|work=[[Wikipedia:The Epoch Times|''The Epoch Times'']]|archivedate=20250420204730}}</ref> made by Lucasfilm's visual effects company [[Industrial Light & Magic]].<ref name="YBY" /> The Soviet Union's [[Wikipedia:Literaturnaya Gazeta|''Literary Gazette'']] in [[Wikipedia:Moscow|Moscow]] likewise disparaged the film on September 7, 1977, describing it as an unsurprisingly profitable horror film where America's "mass audience is ready to bite at such pieces of [[Wikipedia:Scare quotes|“art”]] in order to, when leaving the movie theater, see that things outside are fairly quiet&hellip;"<ref name="Moscow review">{{WebCite|url=https://commons.trincoll.edu/eclectic/first-review-of-star-wars-in-the-soviet-union|text=First Review of Star Wars in the Soviet Union|author=David Chappell|date=2024-05-23|work=[[Wikipedia:Trinity College (Connecticut)|Trinity College (Connecticut)]]|archivedate=20240625014827}}</ref> From 1978, Chinese authorities began promoting science fiction literature in an effort to rehabilitate scientific research<ref name="RFI" /> until the [[Wikipedia:Campaign against spiritual pollution|campaign against "spiritual pollution"]] such as science fiction in 1983.<ref name="NYT China">{{WebCite|url=http://cn.nytstyle.com/culture/20160106/tc06starwar|text=《原力觉醒》：中国星战迷的新希望？|author=Cao Li|date=2016-01-06|work=[[Wikipedia:The New York Times|''The New York Times'']]|archivedate=20160109063312}}</ref>

Although the prequel trilogy released in China and inspired a generation of Chinese ''Star Wars'' fans,<ref name="NYT China" /> the original trilogy was not officially released in China's cinemas until June 2015. In the late 2010s, Lucasfilm made brief forays into officially collaborating with Chinese companies via Disney in the late 2010s with [[Shanghai Disneyland]], the ill-fated ''[[Star Wars: Galactic Conflict]]'' video game, and the web-novel ''[[The Vow of Silver Dawn]]''.<ref name="Cultural Revolution" /> In contrast to [[Wikipedia:Mainland China|mainland China]], ''Star Wars'' was screened in [[Wikipedia:Hong Kong|Hong Kong]], a [[Wikipedia:British Hong Kong|colony of the British Empire]] that not only engaged in [[Wikipedia:Entrepôt|entrepôt]] trade between the West and Communist China but also boasted a booming plastics industry, including manufacturing the first ''Star Wars'' figures during the 1970s and 1980s for England's [[Palitoy]] company and the American [[Kenner Products|Kenner]] company.<ref name="Stevens">{{BookCite|author=Stevens, Craig|title=The ''Star Wars'' Phenomenon in Britain|publisher=McFarland|date=2018|isbn=978-1-4766-6608-2}}</ref>

[[File:LarsHotpot.jpg|thumb|left|320px|A panel of Luke Skywalker's family in a Guangdong comic adaptation of ''Star Wars''.]]
When meeting with Chinese Vice Premier [[Wikipedia:Geng Biao|Geng Biao]] in May 1980, President Carter invited Geng to watch ''The Empire Strikes Back'' with the [[Wikipedia:First family of the United States|first family]]. Carter told the press that "Geng Bao was on the edge of his seat all the way through the movie. Fortunately, he did not ask me for any of the weapons he saw in the movie"&mdash;they shared concerns over the [[Wikipedia:Soviet Union|Soviet Union's]] [[Wikipedia:Soviet–Afghan War|invasion]] of [[Wikipedia:Afghanistan|Afghanistan]] and Vietnam's [[Wikipedia:Cambodian–Vietnamese War|invasion]] of [[Wikipedia:Cambodia|Cambodia]] and discussed military cooperation as part of a new relationship between the US and China.<ref name="YBY" /> In the same year, Chinese officials in [[Wikipedia:Guangzhou|Guangzhou City]] of the [[Wikipedia:Guangdong|Guangdong]] province, which neighbors Hong Kong, obtained a copy of 1977's ''Star Wars'' and summoned the artist Song Feideng (宋飞等) to a private viewing. He was tasked with illustrating a [[Wikipedia:Lianhuanhua|lianhuanhua]] picture book to promote scientific achievement in China. Song found the film exciting and happily received payment for his work, which Zhou Jinzhuo (周金灼) wrote in adaptation of the film and sold quickly under the Guangzhou branch of the Popular Science Press (科学普及出版社). It remained one of the few ''Star Wars'' material in mainland China alongside pirated videos and some broadcasts by local television stations in the late 1980s.<ref name="RFI" /><ref name="RFA">{{OfficialYouTube|video=XmWydHO8bxs|text='The Force' was with China, Already in the 1980s|channel=@rfa|name=[[Wikipedia:Radio Free Asia|Radio Free Asia]]|date=2016-01-22}}</ref><ref name="Chinese comic">{{WebCite|url=https://www.nickstember.com/chinese-star-wars-comic-part-1-6|text=Chinese Star Wars Comic (Part 1 of 6)|author=Nick Stember|date=2014-05-27|work=[https://www.nickstember.com Nick Stember] blog|archivedate=20140529200338}}</ref>

''Star Wars'' lianhuanhua were published by children's book publishers across China, including the regions of [[Wikipedia:Yunnan|Yunnan]] in the southwest, [[Wikipedia:Anhui|Anhui]] in the east, and [[Wikipedia:Inner Mongolia|Inner Mongolia]] in the north.<ref name="More lianhuanhua">{{WebCite|url=https://generationskywalker.com/f/test-blog-post?hcb=1|text=STAR WARS LIANHUANHUA|author=Craig Spivey|date=2020-02-29|work=Generation Skywalker|archivedate=20250406193247}}</ref> In October 1980, in [[Wikipedia:Changsha|Changsha City]] of the [[Wikipedia:Hunan|Hunan]] province north of Guangdong, the local branch of the [[Wikipedia:People's Press (Beijing)|People's Press]] commissioned ''Star Wars'' novellas under the editor and veteran translator Tang Yinsun (唐荫荪) with translators Zhang Ruoheng (张若衡) and Chen Yuequan (陈月泉), firstly adapting [[Donald F. Glut]]'s [[Star Wars: The Empire Strikes Back (novelization)|novelization]] of ''The Empire Strikes Back'' and then the original ''Star Wars'' with other staff. In October 1986, the publisher started printing its adaptation of ''Return of the Jedi'' but was performing poorly, and it switched to printing adaptions of the English erotic novel [[Wikipedia:Lady Chatterley's Lover|''Lady Chatterley's Lover'']], which was hugely popular, banned on the charge of obscenity, and led to the publisher being sanctioned.<ref name="Hunan">{{WebCite|url=https://www.bohaishibei.com/post/77924|text=80 年湖南人民出版社的《星球大战》中译本小说|date=2022-10-20|work=博海拾贝|archivedate=20221025080755}}</ref> Around the same time, manufacturing of ''Star Wars'' toys in Hong Kong began relocating to Guangdong amidst China's [[Wikipedia:Chinese economic reform|economic reforms]] and socio-economic uncertainty over the impending [[Wikipedia:Handover of Hong Kong|transfer]] of sovereignty over Hong Kong to the People's Republic of China in 1997.<ref name="Stevens" />

=====Hong Kong: city on the edge=====
[[File:SeparatistSenate2-HOBS.png|thumb|right|350px|The Separatists in ''The Clone Wars'' and the British Empire&mdash;parliamentary democracy and organs of commerce]]
While local industries declined, Hong Kong refocused on servicing as an international financial center, being situated at the edge of both Western and Chinese influence.<ref name="Stevens" /> In ''Star Wars and History'', Michael Laver compared the nationalization of the [[Wikipedia:Dutch East India Company|Dutch]] and [[Wikipedia:East India Company|British East India Companies]] with the Galactic Empire's absorption of the Trade Federation, stressing the giants of commerce could outlive their usefulness to their respective imperial metropole. Katrina Gulliver also wrote in the book that [[Wikipedia:London|London]], as the world's premier financial center and metropole of opportunity in the heyday of the East India Company, was recognized as a modern version of the past dominant cities of [[Wikipedia:Babylon|Babylon]], [[Wikipedia:Jerusalem|Jerusalem]], and Rome, developing technological and economical innovations fit for empire hand-in-hand with the widely influencial [[Wikipedia:Westminster system|Westminster model]] of parliamentary democracy. Gulliver added that London's leading status passed to [[Wikipedia:New York City|New York]] and the United States in the early 20th century, in contrast to the long-term dominance of Coruscant as capital of the ''Star Wars'' galaxy and Republic.<ref name="History" />

Further back in time and closer to Hong Kong, Laver's essay in ''Star Wars and History'' compares Tatooine and Cloud City in their capacity as ports on the galaxy's political and economic fringe with southeastern Chinese ports such as [[Wikipedia:Xiamen|Amoy]] (Xiamen) and Canton (Guanzhou). They were geographically far from China's capital city of [[Wikipedia:Beijing|Beijing]], and [[Wikipedia:Pirates of the South China Coast|piracy]] thrived in southern China during the eighteenth and nineteenth centuries. In the [[Wikipedia:17th century|17th century]], pirate-admiral [[Wikipedia:Zheng Zhilong|Zheng Zhilong]] and his [[Wikipedia:Ming Dynasty Zheng family|family's]] competed with the collapsing [[Wikipedia:Ming dynasty|Ming]] and rising [[Wikipedia:Qing dynasty|Qing dynasties]] of China&mdash;likened to the fragile Galactic Republic&mdash;as well as a [[Wikipedia:Edo period|Edo Japan]] and the Dutch East India Company (VOC) in [[Wikipedia:Taiwan|Formosa]] (Taiwan), which parallel Lando Calrissian as a pirate, Cloud City's Baron Administrator, and rebel general. Laver also compared the Trade Federation's model of having its own trade fleet with the VOC and the British East India Company as "states within a state" away from the European metropole in the 17th century; just as Viceroy Nute Gunray oversaw the blockade of Naboo, the VOC imposed many blockades on rival imperial powers such as [[Wikipedia:Portuguese India|Portugal]] and [[Wikipedia:Spanish Empire|Spain]] in [[Wikipedia:Goa|Goa]], [[Wikipedia:Portuguese Macau|Macau]], and [[Wikipedia:Manila|Manila]] in southeast Asia, while the East India Company was commissioned in England as Spain lost its [[Wikipedia:Anglo-Spanish War (1585–1604)|war in Europe]] and the company lobbied [[Wikipedia:Parliament of England|Parliament]] to maintain its monopoly of trade in India, eventually incorporating the land as the heart of the British Empire.<ref name="History" />

According to Laver, endemic corruption and a "[[Wikipedia:Divide and rule|divide and conquer]]" policy relating to the Dutch and British imperial powers also mirror the state of the Galactic Republic and the Trade Federation's failure, in the case of the Naboo and the Gungans, to dominate the populations separately. The author stressed that in terms of socioeconomic and political ramifications of corporate barons' ruthless pursuit of profit, the Trade Federation's use of military force to advance economic interests most clearly reflects British imperialist companies' [[Wikipedia:Opium Wars|Opium Wars]] against Qing dynasty China, forcing the [[Wikipedia:Opium|opium]] trade through Canton by [[Wikipedia:Treaty of Nanking|beginning]] the first of many "[[Wikipedia:Unequal treaties|unequal treaties]]" with, among other stipulations favoring British merchants' [[Wikipedia:Free trade|convenience of trade]] in 1842, the ceding of [[Wikipedia:Hong Kong Island|Hong Kong Island]] to [[Wikipedia:Queen Victoria|Queen Victoria]] of [[Wikipedia:United Kingdom of Great Britain and Ireland|Great Britain]].<ref name="History" /> Three special packs of ''Star Wars'' action figures celebrated the transfer of sovereignty over Hong Kong to the People's Republic of China&mdash;under the [[Wikipedia:Sino-British Joint Declaration|Sino-British Joint Declaration]] registered at the United Nations&mdash;on [[July 1]], 1997.<ref name="Handover small">{{Amazon|url=Star-Wars-Villians-Set-Limited/dp/B003M40CZE|text=Star Wars - 1997 Limited Hong Kong Commemorative Edition I - Heroes Edition}}</ref><ref name="Handover large">{{WebCite|url=https://www.ebay.co.uk/itm/275030829308|text=STAR WARS 1997 HONG KONG COMMEMORATIVE 12" CS LUKE, DARTH VADER & OBI-WAN MISB|work=[[Wikipedia:eBay|eBay]]|archivedate=20260202222515}}</ref>

====Slavery and apartheid====
{{Quote|The effect of war on the common people is something that Dave and I wanted to explore from the beginning and George really liked the stories where we got into that. It speaks to what's happening in our world today and I think it gives some emotional reality to the battles that makes us care about the outcomes because the lives of the innocents are hanging in the balance.|Henry Gilroy, speaking in 2008 about the Ryloth trilogy|<ref name="GilroyFiloni-TFN">{{WebCite|url=https://www.theforce.net/jedicouncil/interview/henrygilroyanddavefiloni.asp|text=TheForce.Net - Jedi Council -  Interviews &#124; Henry Gilroy and Dave Filoni|date=2008-12-29|work=[[TheForce.net]]|archivedate=20090103024315}}</ref>}}
[[File:Peace-Trespass.jpg|thumb|left|330px|Peace between the Talz and Pantoran peoples in "Trespass."]]
Named the "Grand Army of the Republic," the fictional clone forces share its name with the United States' [[Wikipedia:Grand Army of the Republic|Grand Army of the Republic]] organization of veterans who had served in the [[Wikipedia:American Civil War|American Civil War]] against the seceding [[Wikipedia:Slave states and free states|slavery-based]] [[Wikipedia:Confederate States of America|Confederate States]]<ref name="History" />&mdash;the fictional Separatist state was identified as the "Confederacy of Independent Systems" in ''Star Wars'' Legends material,<ref name="EGTW">''[[The Essential Guide to Warfare]]''</ref> which was later confirmed by the 2010 "[[Sphere of Influence]]" episode of the ''[[Star Wars: The Clone Wars]]'' television series, created by George Lucas.<ref name="Sphere" /> For the [[2009]] "[[Trespass]]" episode of ''The Clone Wars'', focusing on the Pantorans' war against the Talz, voice actor [[Brian George]]'s performance as Chairman Chi Cho drew on "some of the uglier voices of the [[Wikipedia:South Africa|South African]] [[Wikipedia:Apartheid|apartheid]] era" that only ended in the 1990s and the "venom and hatred that we used to hear on the news every night." George also likened his character's hatefulness to [[Wikipedia:Bosnian genocide|ethnic cleansing]] in the former Yugoslavia, the [[1994]] [[Wikipedia:Rwandan genocide|Rwandan genocide]], the [[Wikipedia:Darfur genocide|Darfur genocide]] between 2003 and 2005, and tragically, many more.<ref name="Brian George">{{SWArchive|url=theclonewars/news/20090129.html|text=Chairman Cho: A Very Bad Man}}</ref>

[[File:Nabat.png|thumb|right|350px|The cost of war in ''The Clone Wars''{{'}} Ryloth arc, partly based on the tragedies of Afghanistan]]
[[Henry Gilroy]] wrote the 2009 Ryloth trilogy of ''The Clone Wars'' while thinking about Afghanistan, particularly on how it was invaded by Soviet Russia and the United States supplied the [[Wikipedia:Afghan mujahideen|Afghan mujahideen]] with weapons to counter the Soviets, and some of the [[Wikipedia:Afghans|Afghans]] eventually fought American forces in another [[Wikipedia:War in Afghanistan (2001–2021)|war]] that started in 2001 as part of the war on terror. The first and second episodes, "[[Storm Over Ryloth]] and "[[Innocents of Ryloth]]," were partly based on battles of the Second World War.<ref name="Gilroy podcast">{{WebCite|url=https://www.spreaker.com/episode/issue-181-henry-gilroy-the-ryloth-arc--19301238|text=Issue 181: Henry Gilroy: The Ryloth Arc|date=2019-10-01|work=Spreaker|archivedate=20260118224015}}</ref> According to lead character designer [[Kilian Plunkett]], Twi'lek culture was based on the north African nations of [[Wikipedia:Algeria|Algeria]] and [[Wikipedia:Morocco|Morocco]].<ref name="Art of TCW">''[[The Art of Star Wars: The Clone Wars]]''</ref> The writers took inspiration from the French Revolution for the third episode, "[[Liberty on Ryloth]]," which also introduced Cham Syndulla. The character was changed from a Separatist agent lying to his people to a freedom fighter to better suit the plot, and, given that George Lucas had insisted that Twi'leks speak in French accents, executive producer [[Dave Filoni]] referred to him as the leader of the [[Wikipedia:French Resistance|French Resistance]] of the Second World War.<ref name="Liberty featurette">{{SW|url=video/liberty-on-ryloth-episode-featurette|text="Liberty on Ryloth" Episode Featurette}}</ref> [[Catherine Taber]], who voiced Numa in the second episode, thought that the history of the Twi'leks shared a cultural thread with the [[Wikipedia:Irish people|Irish]] people.<ref name="Taber">{{WebCite|url=https://www.geeksundergrace.com/movies/interview-cat-taber|text=Interview: Cat Taber|author=David Austin Black|date=2015-08-14|work=Geeks Under Grace|archivedate=20150904045642}}</ref>

In the book ''Star Wars and History'', Paul Horvath and Mark Higbee compared the economic basis of the ''Star Wars'' Confederacy's leadership&mdash;battle droid manufacturers such as the Trade Federation&mdash;with the economic basis of the American Confederacy&mdash;[[Wikipedia:Slave plantation|cotton plantations]] reliant on enslaved labor. Economic interests, coupled with political change&mdash;either the despotic Palpatine's rise as Chancellor of the Republic in ''Star Wars'' or the anti-slavery [[Wikipedia:Abraham Lincoln|Abraham Lincoln's]] [[Wikipedia:1860 United States presidential election|election]] as President the United States in 1860&mdash;contributed to the outbreak of civil war, with the summary executions of Jedi echoing the Confederate [[Wikipedia:Battle of Fort Pillow|massacre at Fort Pillow]] of surrendering Black US soldiers. The Galactic Empire's promises of peace, violated particularly by the oppression of non-human species until the galaxy's liberation by the Rebel Alliance, was also compared with the post-[[Wikipedia:Reconstruction era|Reconstruction]] decades of America's [[Wikipedia:Nadir of American race relations|nadir of race relations]] and the struggle of modern American civil rights campaigners.<ref name="History" />

[[File:Anakinandmom.jpg|thumb|left|320px|The tragedy of Anakin Skywalker]]
Paul Finkelmen wrote in ''Star Wars and History'' that bondage existed in various forms throghout human history, from legal forced labor and concubinage in [[Wikipedia:Mesopotamia|ancient Mesopotamia]]; descriptions in the [[Wikipedia:Bible|Bible]]; and ancient Greece and Rome when limited democratic enfranchisement existed, to transactional chattel slavery in the [[Wikipedia:Atlantic slave trade|transatlantic slave trade]]; state institutions of [[Wikipedia:Serfdom|serfdom]], [[Wikipedia:Gulag|gulag]] systems, and concentration camps; and human trafficking in the modern era. Finkelmen compared slavery's role in separating affected family members with the emotional toll of Anakin Skywalker having to leave his still-enslaved mother behind on Tatooine at a young age, akin to American abolitionist [[Wikipedia:Frederick Douglass|Frederick Douglass']] childhood, with Anakin having to carry emotional scars that ultimately prevent him from fulfilling his duties as a Jedi. Finkelmen also compared Luke Slywalker's sense of being held back by his family on Tatooine with Abraham Lincoln's restrictive childhood. Although Tatooine, a world "beyond the line" of Western civilization yet one where Padmé Amidala is shocked to find slavery given the Republic's outlawing of slavery, and particularly Jabba's Palace evokes [[Wikipedia:Orientalism|Orientalist]] connotations of moral corruption and sexual exploitation in [[Wikipedia:Mali Empire|Mali]], [[Wikipedia:Ottoman Empire|Ottoman]], or Indian empires, Finkelmen added that Leia Organa's slaying of Jabba the Hutt echoes the [[Wikipedia:Haitian Revolution|Haitian Revolution]] in the 1790s, where the "slave" became the agent of their own destiny and massacred their "masters." Highlighting enslavement being the first listed human rights violation in 1948's [[Wikipedia:Universal Declaration of Human Rights|Universal Declaration of Human Rights]] in the context of [[Wikipedia:Final Solution|pan-European]] German extermination camps and slave labor battalions and [[Wikipedia:Greater East Asia Co-Prosperity Sphere|pan-Asian]] Japanese [[Wikipedia:List of Japanese-run internment camps during World War II|internment camps]] and [[Wikipedia:Comfort women|forced prostitution]] from the early 1930s to 1945, Finkelmen's analysis identified the clone troopers and stormtroopers also as enslaved soldiers and brainwashed cult members, with mindless droid armies being the "ultimate slaves," enforcing pan-galactic bondage of certain despots over others.<ref name="History" />

====Feminism====
{{Quote|I think [Padmé] embodies the true meaning of feminism in my understanding of it. It's about having access to all the same things but then making decisions from your particular point of view&hellip;[Padmé] has been a leader of many people, but rather than being consumed with the thirst for power&hellip;she stays true to her compassion and her belief in democracy and in humanity&hellip;|Natalie Portman, speaking during the filming of ''Revenge of the Sith'' in 2003|<ref name="Portman">{{Facebook|username=lucasfilm|url=lucasfilm/photos/i-think-padmé-embodies-the-true-meaning-of-feminism-in-my-understanding-of-it-sa/3054828551241823|name=[[Lucasfilm]]|date=2020-03-20|quote="I think [Padmé] embodies the true meaning of feminism in my understanding of it," said Natalie Portman during filming of "Revenge of the Sith" in 2003. "It's about having access to all the same things but then making decisions from your particular point of view&hellip;[Padmé] has been a leader of many people, but rather than being consumed with the thirst for power...she stays true to her compassion and her belief in democracy and in humanity..."|archiveurl=https://archive.ph/XnqUH}}</ref>}}
[[File:Queenamidala.jpg|thumb|right|300px|Natalie Portman as Padmé Amidala, Queen of Naboo]]
While filming in [[2003]] for [[Star Wars: Episode III Revenge of the Sith|''Star Wars'': Episode III ''Revenge of the Sith'']]&mdash;the final installment of the prequel trilogy which was released in [[2005]]&mdash;Padmé Amidala's actor, [[Natalie Portman]], said that her character embodies "the true meaning of [[Wikipedia:Feminism|feminism]] in my understanding of it" in accessing "all the same things" while making her own decisions and leading many people while remaining steadfast to her values of compassion, democracy, and belief in humanity.<ref name="Portman" /> In 2012's ''Star Wars and History'', Janice Liedl compared Queen Amidala's fight for her people's freedom to Queen [[Wikipedia:Boudica|Boudicca]], who led the [[Wikipedia:Celtic Britons|Celtic Britons']] [[Wikipedia:Boudican revolt|revolt]] against the occupying Romans. Janice Liedl and Nancy R. Reagin added that women have been "integral to every modern revolution," likening the critical leadership roles of Princess Leia Organa and Ahsoka Tano&mdash;a protagonist of ''Star Wars: The Clone Wars''&mdash;with actions of real-world women that could be considered "unfeminine," such as the teenage [[Wikipedia:Joan of Arc|Joan of Arc]] leading the [[Wikipedia:Kingdom of France|French]] to end the [[Wikipedia:England|English]] [[Wikipedia:Siege of Orléans|Siege of Orléans]] in 1429; [[Wikipedia:Charlotte Corday|Charlotte Corday's]] assassination of the influencial writer [[Wikipedia:Jean-Paul Marat|Jean-Paul Marat]] after the [[Wikipedia:September Massacres|September 1792 massacres]] of the French Revolution; the Irish revolutionary [[Wikipedia:Constance Markievicz|Constance Markievicz's]] election to the [[Wikipedia:Parliament of the United Kingdom|UK Parliament]] in 1918 and the [[Wikipedia:Irish Republic|Irish Republic's]] [[Wikipedia:First Dáil|first Dáil Éireann]] assembly in the following year; and [[Wikipedia:Dora María Téllez|Dora María Téllez's]] becoming a military leader and spokesperson of the [[Wikipedia:Nicaraguan Revolution|Nicaraguan Revolution]] in 1979.<ref name="History" />

Liedl and Reagin further noted that a hairstyle worn by woman supporting the [[Wikipedia:Mexican Revolution|Mexican Revolution]] of 1910&ndash;1920 also helped inspire Princess Leia Organa's iconic hairstyle in ''A New Hope'', and the [[Wikipedia:July Revolution|1830 French Revolution]] painting [[Wikipedia:Liberty Leading the People|''Liberty Leading the People'']] features a woman, [[Wikipedia:Marianne|Marianne]], symbolizing [[Wikipedia:France|France]] and [[Wikipedia:Liberté, égalité, fraternité|liberty]]. Liedl further wrote that Padmé Amidala's funeral and the palpably "powerful influence" on those who shared her ideals of the Republic echoes the [[Wikipedia:Death and state funeral of Liliʻuokalani|funeral]] of [[Wikipedia:Hawaiian Kingdom|Hawaiian Queen]] [[Wikipedia:Liliʻuokalani|Liliʻuokalani]], associated with the indigenous culture of [[Wikipedia:Hawaii|Hawaii]] following the islands' settlement by the United States. The historian also remarked that Princess Leia Organa's "hands-on role" reflects the leadership of Queens [[Wikipedia:Elizabeth I|Elizabeth I]] and [[Wikipedia:Elizabeth II|Elizabeth II]] during the Anglo-Spanish War and the Second World War, respectively.<ref name="History" /> Feminism remains a pertinent theme in ''Star Wars'' films beyond George Lucas' time in Lucasfilm.<ref name="Laura Dern VF">{{WebCite|url=https://www.vanityfair.com/hollywood/2017/12/laura-dern-star-wars-last-jedi-admiral-holdo|text=Laura Dern on Leading the Resistance, On-Screen and Off|author=Olivia Aylmer|date=2017-12-15|work=[[Wikipedia:Vanity Fair (magazine)|''Vanity Fair'']]|archivedate=20201111193001}}</ref>

===="Mutually assured destruction" and the war on terror====
{{Quote|Fear is the path to the dark side. Fear leads to anger; anger leads to hate; hate leads to suffering.|Master Yoda|<ref name="TPM" />}}
[[File:Lucasfilm_Archives.jpg|thumb|left|280px|"Fear will keep the local systems in line. Fear of this battle station."&mdash;Grand Moff Tarkin in ''A New Hope'']]
The [[Wikipedia:Pacific War|Pacific Theater]] of the Second World War ended in 1945 with Imperial Japan [[Wikipedia:Surrender of Japan|unconditional surrender]] following the Allied Powers' [[Wikipedia:Manhattan Project|invention]] of the [[Wikipedia:Nuclear weapon|atomic bomb]] and the US' [[Wikipedia:Atomic bombings of Hiroshima and Nagasaki|two detonations over Hiroshima and Nagasaki]]. With the Soviet Union's [[Wikipedia:RDS-1|first nuclear bomb test]] in 1949, the Communist victory in the [[Wikipedia:Chinese Civil War|Chinese Civil War]] later that year, and the Soviet launch of [[Wikipedia:Sputnik crisis|Sputnik]] in the [[Wikipedia:Space Race|Space Race]] in 1957 fuelled worries that a [[Wikipedia:Nuclear arms race|nuclear arms race]] between the Soviet and American superpowers would lead to [[Wikipedia:Mutual assured destruction|mutually assured annihilation]], reflected in American films such as Stanley Kubrick's 1964 ''Dr. Strangelove or: How I Learned to Stop Worrying and Love the Bomb'',<ref name="History" /> which influenced George Lucas.<ref name="Cannes 2024" /> Writing in 2012's ''Star Wars and History'', Lori Maguire noted Lucas' dislike of US President Nixon: despite the 1970s' détente with the Soviet Union led by Nixon and advisor [[Wikipedia:Henry Kissinger|Henry Kissinger]], revelations about dishonesty over the Vietnam War from [[Wikipedia:Presidency of Lyndon B. Johnson|Lyndon B. Johnson's presidency]], Nixon's [[Wikipedia:Watergate scandal|Watergate scandal]], and [[Wikipedia:Operation Menu|secret bombings]] of Cambodia undermined public confidence in the US government, and the Death Star in ''Star Wars''{{'}} original trilogy resonated with the [[Wikipedia:Cold War|Cold War]] reality at the time.<ref name="History" />

[[File:Mandalore_mural.jpg|thumb|right|300px|The Mandalorian mural inspired by Picasso's ''Guernica''.]]
When US President [[Wikipedia:Ronald Reagan|Ronald Reagan]] proposed a space-based missile defense system called the [[Wikipedia:Strategic Defense Initiative|Strategic Defense Initiative]] in 1983, Senator [[Wikipedia:Ted Kennedy|Ted Kennedy]] criticized this "''Star Wars'' scheme" as "a trip wire for nuclear war" based on a "preposterous" romanticization of the [[Wikipedia:American frontier|American West]]. The "''Star Wars''" nickname was then popularized by the media<ref name="History" /> despite Lucasfilm requesting news bureaus not to.<ref name="YBY" /> The [[Wikipedia:United States Space Force|US Space Force]] was founded in [[2019]] and began developing the [[Wikipedia:Golden Dome (missile defense system)|Golden Dome]] missile defense system in 2025 primarily to counter China.<ref name="Golden Dome">{{WebCite|url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/news/articles/cy8k2nd7e9no|text='Sparring in space' – BBC gains rare access to US base tracking global missile strikes|date=2025-07-16|work=[[Wikipedia:BBC|BBC]]|archivedate=20250716173148}}</ref>

Maguire noted that fear had been used as an effective weapon in war, such as fascist German and Italian forces' [[Wikipedia:Bombing of Guernica|bombing of Guernica]] in 1937 to support [[Wikipedia:Francisco Franco|Francisco Franco]] in the [[Wikipedia:Spanish Civil War|Spanish Civil War]].<ref name="History" /> The 2010 [[The Clone Wars: Season Two|Season Two]] episode "[[The Mandalore Plot]]" of ''The Clone Wars'' and the [[2013]] [[The Clone Wars: Season Five|Season Five]] episodes "[[Shades of Reason]]" and "[[The Lawless]]" feature cubist murals of Mandalorians killing Jedi, which were inspired by the artist [[Wikipedia:Pablo Picasso|Pablo Picasso]] and specifically [[Wikipedia:Guernica (Picasso)|''Guernica'']], painted in response to the bombing of that [[Wikipedia:Basque Country (autonomous community)|Basque]] town.<ref name="Guernica Filoni">{{SW|url=video/the-mandalore-plot-episode-featurette|text="The Mandalore Plot" Episode Featurette}}</ref><ref name="Shades Gallery">{{SW|url=series/clone-wars/shades-of-reason-trivia-gallery|text="Shades of Reason" Trivia Gallery}} {{C|Slides 3 and 4}}</ref> Maguire also noted that fear could be used to start wars, such as the US and UK's claims of [[Wikipedia:Weapon of mass destruction|weapons of mass destruction]] in [[Wikipedia:Iraq|Iraq]] as rationale for the [[Wikipedia:2003 invasion of Iraq|2003 invasion]] during the war on terror.<ref name="History" />

[[File:SW_prequel_poster.jpg|thumb|left|360px|The prequel trilogy was conceived during the Vietnam War but Lucas also compared the story to his concerns for American democracy during the Iraq War.]]
Lucas acknowledged at the [[Wikipedia:Cannes Film Festival|Cannes Film Festival]] in 2005 that the prequel trilogy's tale of democracy's collapse is comparable to the United States' then-ongoing [[Wikipedia:Iraq War|war in Iraq]],<ref name="Cannes 2005" /> and according to journalist [[Wikipedia:Maureen Dowd|Maureen Dowd]] in [[2009]], Lucas also compared Anakin Skywalker to [[Wikipedia:George W. Bush|George W. Bush]] who oversaw the war as US President and the role of the mentor, Emperor Palpatine, to Bush's [[Wikipedia:Vice President of the United States|Vice President]], [[Wikipedia:Dick Cheney|Dick Cheney]].<ref name="Cheney">{{WebCite|url=https://www.nytimes.com/2009/04/19/opinion/19dowd.html|text=The Aura of Arugulance|author=Maureen Dowd|date=2009-04-18|work=[[Wikipedia:The New York Times|''The New York Times'']]|archivedate=20130615063221}}</ref> However, Lucas had clarified in 2005 that he had written the prequel story during the Vietnam War, with concerns about the [[Wikipedia:Kennedy family|Kennedy family]] and President Nixon and his worry that democracy in America is vulnerable&mdash;he believed that politicians' inefficiency prompts voters to elect charismatic "tyrants" who promised to "get the job done."<ref name="Cannes 2005" /> In his 1999 interview with Schell, Lucas had said that "a benevolent despot is the ideal ruler" to "get things done," with the caveat that "power corrupts" and only "a big human" could overcome power abuse.<ref name="Cynical optimism" />

William J. Astore wrote in 2012's ''Star Wars and History'' that the United States was effectively conflicted between the roles of benevolent Jedi&mdash;some American units were praised as "Jedi" for their service the wars against the Iraqi despot [[Wikipedia:Saddam Hussein|Saddam Hussein]]&mdash;and imperialist Sith, given the civilian casualties and collateral damage incurred during both the Iraq War and the then-ongoing war in Afghanistan.<ref name="History" /> The conflict ended with the botched [[Wikipedia:2020–2021 U.S. troop withdrawal from Afghanistan|American-led withdrawal]] at [[Wikipedia:Kabul|Kabul]] and Afghanistan's fall to the [[Wikipedia:Taliban|Taliban]] regime in [[2021]].<ref name="Fall of Kabul">{{WebCite|url=https://hansard.parliament.uk/Commons/2021-08-18/debates/A86142BD-A204-4BC8-BBC0-ACA7BAD7E9F0/Afghanistan|text=Afghanistan|date=2021-08-18|work=[[Wikipedia:House of Commons of the United Kingdom|Hansard, House of Commons (UK)]]|archivedate=20240729191445}}</ref> A US Marine and ''Star Wars'' fan killed while defending the [[Wikipedia:2021 Kabul airlift|Kabul airlift]], Corporal Hunter Lopez, was officially honored at [[Disneyland]] in [[Wikipedia:Anaheim, California|Anaheim, California]] by Disney and his family alongside his handbuilt lightsaber from its ''[[Star Wars: Galaxy's Edge]]'' themed land.<ref name="Lopez">{{WebCite|url=https://www.ocregister.com/2021/09/20/disneyland-pays-tribute-to-fallen-u-s-marine-killed-in-afghanistan-airport-attack|text=Disneyland helps fulfill fallen Marine’s wish of being buried with lightsaber|author=Brady MacDonald|date=2021-09-20|work=[[Wikipedia:The Orange County Register|''The Orange County Register'']]|archivedate=20221130193037}}</ref>

===Beyond George Lucas===
====Transition to Disney====
{{Quote|And George, you know, his increasing need to use politics as a way to express some of his frustration with what was going on in the world is what comes through in the Star Wars [prequels]. And, you know, fans can look at that and determine whether or not they connected with that or not, but certainly George did. That was really important to him. And, you know, that was his prerogative. It was his story. He was using that in a way that was personally meaningful to him.|Kathleen Kennedy, in an interview about her approach to encouraging creative freedom in Lucasfilm following Lucas' departure|<ref name="Kathleen TFA">{{WebCite|url=http://www.vanityfair.com/hollywood/2015/05/kathleen-kennedy-star-wars-interview|text=Kathleen Kennedy talks Star Wars: The Force Awakens|author=Bruce Handy|date=2015-05-20|work=[[Wikipedia:Vanity Fair (magazine)|''Vanity Fair'']]|archivedate=20150523010722}}</ref>}}
[[File:LucasTuskegee.jpg|thumb|right|350px|George Lucas (center) in 2012, at the Tuskegee Airmen convention]]
Just as Lucas had created ''Star Wars'' to address themes about human societies without explicitly referencing issues on which audiences have formed their own prejudices of opinion,<ref name="Wired" /> the filmmaker had ironically become frustrated with ''Star Wars'' fans on the [[Wikipedia:Internet|Internet]] who constantly "yell" about "what a terrible person you are" after developing their own prejudices of opinion on what ''Star Wars'' should be.<ref name="Yell">{{WebCite|url=https://www.nytimes.com/2012/01/22/magazine/george-lucas-red-tails.html|text=George Lucas Is Ready to Roll the Credits|author=Bryan Curtis|date=2012-01-17|work=[[Wikipedia:The New York Times|''The New York Times'']]|archivedate=20250321053346}}</ref> Following Lucasfilm's release of the 2012 film [[Wikipedia:Red Tails|''Red Tails'']] about the [[Wikipedia:Tuskegee Airmen|Tuskegee Airmen]] serving in World War II, George Lucas sold his company to [[The Walt Disney Company]] so that Lucasfilm could continue running ''Star Wars'' while he himself focused on experimental filmmaking beyond the "white slaver" mainstream of the [[Wikipedia:Cinema of the United States|Hollywood industry]]<ref name="Charlie Rose full" /> that did not believe films starring [[Wikipedia:Black people|Black people]] had a profitable market outside the United States.<ref name="Tuskegee">{{WebCite|url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/news/entertainment-arts-16525977|text=George Lucas says Hollywood won't support black films|date=2012-01-12|work=[[Wikipedia:BBC|BBC]]|archivedate=20250910193700}}</ref> Lucas had been planning to return to the independent filmmaking of his college days since at least 2005.<ref name="Boston Lucas" />

The book ''Star Wars and History'' was published in late 2012, five years after George Lucas had read a copy of Kevin S. Decker and Jason T. Eberl's ''Star Wars and Philosophy'', which was published independent of Lucasfilm, and asked [[Jonathan W. Rinzler]]&mdash;the author of ''[[The Making of Star Wars: The Definitive Story Behind the Original Film]]''&mdash;to arrange a similar volume of essays by relevant academics on ''Star Wars''{{'}} historical influences.
