Teases Season 2 Filled With Rebel Gangsters & Outliers|author=Anthony D'Alessandro|date=2022-11-23|work=[[Wikipedia:Deadline Hollywood|''Deadline'']]|archivedate=20250102161625}}</ref> and the [[Wikipedia:October Revolution|Russian Revolution]]&mdash;Gilroy wanted every character to "speak for themselves" to make their motives clear.<ref name="Themes" /> Responding to a comparison with Russia's contemporaneous war against Ukraine, Gilroy remarked on how sad it is that terrible events continue to repeat themselves through history.<ref name="Andor finale Deadline" /> The [[Rix Road (episode)|season one finale]] took inspiration from the 1966 [[Wikipedia:Gillo Pontecorvo|Gillo Pontecorvo]] film [[Wikipedia:The Battle of Algiers|''The Battle of Algiers'']], which was based on the [[Wikipedia:Algerian War|Algerian War of Independence]] against the colonial [[Wikipedia:French Fourth Republic|French government]].<ref name="Algiers">{{WebCite|url=https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/movies/movie-features/sharper-director-benjamin-caron-apple-movie-andor-1235356365|text=Sharper Director Benjamin Caron Talks Twisty Apple Movie, Andor|author=Brian Davids|date=2023-03-20|work=[[Wikipedia:The Hollywood Reporter|The Hollywood Reporter]]|archivedate=20230320182210}}</ref>

[[File:MonVerite.jpg|thumb|left|260px|[[Tracie Ching]]'s ''Mon! Verité!'' poster of Mon Mothma speaking her truth]]
Gilroy also likened ''Andor''{{'s}} character of Luthen Rael as a [[Wikipedia:Startup company|start-up company]] founder<ref name="Themes" /> and, earlier in September, identified the senator and eventual Rebel Alliance leader Mon Mothma as a "figurehead of liberal democracy that will fail."<ref name="Mothma">{{WebCite|url=https://www.vanityfair.com/hollywood/2022/09/mon-mothma-andor-star-wars|text=Who Is Mon Mothma? Star Wars Fans Are About To Be Shocked|author=Anthony Breznican|date=2022-09-27|work=[[Wikipedia:Vanity Fair (magazine)|''Vanity Fair'']]|archivedate=20221128144344}}</ref> Gilroy compared the pre-existing canon role of her as a "still portrait" of a "proper" and "powerful" senator like [[Wikipedia:Speaker of the United States House of Representatives|US House Speaker]] [[Wikipedia:Nancy Pelosi|Nancy Pelosi]] in another interview in the same month and promised that ''Andor'' would "build a real life" behind Mothma's character,<ref name="Pelosi">{{WebCite|url=https://www.indiewire.com/features/general/andor-syril-karn-mon-mothma-1234764813|text=Tony Gilroy Unpacks 'Andor' Ep. 4: Mon Mothma Is 'a Nancy Pelosi Character'|author=Proma Khosla|date=2022-09-28|work=[[Wikipedia:IndieWire|''IndieWire'']]|archivedate=20230507110140}}</ref> and in an interview released in November, he clarified that he was not thinking about Pelosi when writing the scripts.<ref name="Andor finale Deadline" /> [[Genevieve O'Reilly]], the [[Wikipedia:Irish Australians|Irish-Australian]] actor of Mothma in ''Revenge of the Sith'' and ''Andor'', said in an April 2025 interview that she and Gilroy had talked about not placing Mothma in another expositional role again but instead bring a "deeply human" characterisation of the woman to the fore.<ref name="Deeply human">{{WebCite|url=https://www.theguardian.com/tv-and-radio/2025/apr/19/andor-season-two-star-wars-disney-genevieve-oreilly-mon-mothma|text=‘It feels deeply human’: Andor’s Genevieve O’Reilly on turning a tiny Star Wars role into one of its biggest|author=Walter Marsh|date=2025-04-19|work=[[Wikipedia:The Guardian|''The Guardian'']]|archivedate=20250422131529}}</ref>

O'Reilly added that "the tentacles of empire" underpins a large part of human history across the continents and ages, whether it be in Australia, Ireland, America, [[Wikipedia:Africa|Africa]], [[Wikipedia:Portuguese Empire|Portugal]], or France.<ref name="Deeply human" /> Speaking in the following month, O'Reilly said that to develop her portrayal of Mon Mothma, she was inspired by many female leaders and rebels in history&mdash;among them [[Wikipedia:Zoe Porphyrogenita|Empress Zoe]] of the [[Wikipedia:Byzantine Empire|Roman Empire in Constantinople]] and the Irish revolutionary politician Constance Markievicz and also the contemporary [[Wikipedia:President of Mexico|President of Mexico]] [[Wikipedia:Claudia Sheinbaum|Claudia Sheinbaum]].<ref name="LA Times Espanol">{{YouTube|video=5bKw6GNkOL8|text=Tony Gilroy, Ben Mendelsohn y Genevieve O'Reilly hablan de los latinos en ANDOR y del final del show|channel=c/LosAngelesTimesEnEspañol|name=[[Wikipedia:Los Angeles Times|Los Angeles Times En Español]]|date=2025-06-04}}</ref> The character of Mon Mothma in relation to the real world had also been previously discussed by authors of official ''Star Wars'' material. In 2012's ''Star Wars and History'', Janice Liedl had likened Mon Mothma to [[Wikipedia:Matilda of Tuscany|Countess Matilda of Tuscany]], who cut off her [[Wikipedia:Godfrey the Hunchback|husband]] from her possessions and [[Wikipedia:Road to Canossa|brokered]] a brief peace between the [[Wikipedia:Henry IV, Holy Roman Emperor|Holy Roman Emperor]] and the [[Wikipedia:Pope Gregory VII|Pope]], whom she supported.<ref name="History" /> In February 2017, [[Chuck Wendig]], author of the 2015&ndash;2017 [[Star Wars: The Aftermath Trilogy|''The Aftermath Trilogy'']] which detailed Mon Mothma leading the fledgling New Republic, had described her as a "fairly centrist leader" who proved the difficulty of good governance.<ref name="Wendig Empire's End">{{SW|url=news/chuck-wendig-interview-aftermath-empires-end|text=Chuck Wendig on the Battle of Jakku, Han and Leia's Complex Relationship, and More from ''Aftermath: Empire's End''}}</ref> 

[[Fiona Shaw]], who played Maarva Andor and spoke in an interview released in August 2022, thought that Gilroy's story is a "scurrilous" and "[[Wikipedia:Social realism|social-realist]]" take on "the [[Wikipedia:Trumpism|Trumpian]] world" that was "exploding in different places right now" with people's rights being taken away.<ref name="Trumpian world" /> Gilroy said that Syril Karn, played by [[Kyle Soller]], was aspiring to be part of the Imperial Security Bureau, which he compared to the Nazi [[Wikipedia:Schutzstaffel|SS]].<ref name="Pelosi" /> In April 2025, Soller said that his character showed the "banality of evil"&mdash;a concept from [[Wikipedia:Hannah Arendt|Hannah Arendt's]] [[Wikipedia:Eichmann in Jerusalem|1963 report]] on the [[Wikipedia:Eichmann trial|trial]] in Israel of the SS member and Holocaust organizer [[Wikipedia:Adolf Eichmann|Adolf Eichmann]], who was apparently leading an ordinary life in Argentina after the world war&mdash;a unexceptional bureaucrat "desperate to be loved" who would facilitate atrocities for professional promotion.<ref name="Banality of evil">{{WebCite|url=https://www.backstage.com/uk/magazine/article/kyle-soller-andor-interview-78567|text=‘Andor’ Star Kyle Soller on How Failing Made Him a Better Actor|author=[[Kyle Soller]]|date=2025-04-17|work=[[Wikipedia:Backstage (magazine)|''Backstage'']]|archiveurl=https://ghostarchive.org/archive/RjUAf}}</ref>

====Legacies of war, colonialism, and authoritarianism in animation====
[[File:DarkInk-OneHeartDivided.png|thumb|right|240px|[[Ksenia Zelentsova]]'s "One Heart Divided" painting of the clones starring in ''The Bad Batch'']]
In 2020, George Lucas and Dave Filoni reflected on how the clone trooper&ndash;focused stories of ''The Clone Wars'' were very well received by military families.<ref name="SWS 20200430" /> The 2021&ndash;2024 television series ''[[Star Wars: The Bad Batch]]'' follows a group of clones handling the Republic's transition into the Empire. StarWars.com interviewer [[Dan Brooks]] thought that the conflict between Hunter and Crosshair&mdash;without the typical clone inhibitor chip&mdash;represented an ideological struggle between democracy and loyalty to authority, and supervising director and executive producer [[Brad Rau]] replied that he and the series writers wanted to show clone soldiers having different points of views with their own free will, and rather than present a binary view of morality, they wanted the audience to see that "neither one of them were necessarily right or wrong." Head writer and executive producer [[Jennifer Corbett]] added that ''The Bad Batch'' explores how even for ordinary clones, despite the chips' effects "wearing off," some choose to remain with the Empire while others choose not to.<ref name="TBB S1 interview">{{SW|url=news/the-bad-batch-series-end-interview|text=Brad Rau and Jennifer Corbett Look Back on ''Star Wars: The Bad Batch''}}</ref> For [[The Bad Batch Season 3|season 3]], Corbett said the writers intended to ground their stories to real-world topics and particularly focused on military veterans who "no longer have a war to fight" and find themselves unemployed and in need of support.<ref name="TBB S3 interview">{{SW|url=news/the-bad-batch-series-end-interview|text=Brad Rau and Jennifer Corbett Look Back on ''Star Wars: The Bad Batch''}}</ref> The television series ''Ahsoka'', created by Dave Filoni, addresses the question of history repeating in cycles through the character of [[Baylan Skoll]], who says that history "repeats again, and again, and again" in an "inevitable" circle if not stopped.<ref name="Ahsoka 6">{{Ahsoka|Part Six: Far, Far Away}}</ref>

The May 2023 ''[[Star Wars: Visions]]'' animated short "[[In the Stars]]," made by the Chilean studio [[Punkrobot Studios]] and set on a world ravaged by the Galactic Empire for its natural resources, took inspiration from the art and culture of the indigenous peoples of [[Wikipedia:Patagonia|Patagonia]] in southern [[Wikipedia:Chile|Chile]], using the Empire as an allegory for the centuries of colonial oppression imposed on native people by the Spanish Empire and independent Chile, which did not [[Wikipedia:Chilean transition to democracy|transition]] into a modern democracy until dictator [[Wikipedia:Augusto Pinochet|Augusto Pinochet]] was forced to step down in 1990.<ref name="Stars Focus">{{VisionsFocus|In the Stars}}</ref> The short specifically honored the [[Wikipedia:Selk'nam|Selk'nam]] people, against whom the Chilean state conducted [[Wikipedia:Selknam genocide|genocide]].<ref name="Osorio">{{SW|url=news/star-wars-visions-in-the-stars-interview|text=Meet the Visionaries: Gabriel Osorio on the Rich Textures of "In the Stars"}}</ref> The ''Visions'' short "[[The Spy Dancer]]," made by the French [[Studio La Cachette]] and set on an affluent world occupied by the Galactic Empire, was inspired by cabarets in Nazi-occupied Paris as well as the performers [[Wikipedia:Josephine Baker|Josephine Baker]]&mdash;who aided the French Resistance in the Second World War and later the American civil rights movement&mdash;and [[Wikipedia:Mata Hari|Mata Hari]]&mdash;who was executed by [[Wikipedia:French Third Republic|France]] for allegedly spying for the German Empire in the First World War. Director [[Julien Chheng]] was also influenced by his family's history under the genocidal Communist [[Wikipedia:Khmer Rouge|Khmer Rouge]] in 1970s Cambodia, particularly how the regime turned family members against each other. "The Spy Dancer" was particularly well-received by audiences in Cambodia and Argentina, which ended its last [[Wikipedia:National Reorganization Process|military dictatorship]] in 1983 after losing the [[Wikipedia:Falklands War|Falklands War]] to the United Kingdom.<ref name="Spy subtext">{{SW|url=news/star-wars-visions-the-spy-dancer-interview|text=Meet the Visionaries: Julien Chheng on the Subtext of "The Spy Dancer"}}</ref>

[[File:LoieStormtroopers-TheSpyDancer.png|thumb|left|360px|Lucasfilm invited animation studios worldwide to produce ''Star Wars: Visions'' shorts, with some creators drawing on the heritage of dictatorship.]]
Another ''Visions'' short, "[[The Bandits of Golak]]," made by the [[Wikipedia:India|Indian]] studio [[88 Pictures]] and set amidst conflict between the Galactic Empire and native people, serves as a metaphor for the legacy of British colonization and imperialism&mdash;particularly mass displacement and religious divides caused by the [[Wikipedia:Partition of India|Partition of India]] in 1947 in the modern-day states of India, [[Wikipedia:Pakistan|Pakistan]], and [[Wikipedia:Bangladesh|Bangladesh]].<ref name="Partition">{{WebCite|url=https://www.filmcompanion.in/interviews/hollywood-interview/star-wars-how-the-first-indian-entry-to-the-franchise-was-made-star-wars-visions-season-2-disney-hotstar-the-bandits-of-golak|text=How The First Indian Entry To The Star Wars Franchise Was Made|author=Aniruddho Chakraborty|date=2023-05-04|work=[https://www.filmcompanion.in Film Companion]|archivedate=20240713125919}}</ref> The ''Visions'' short "[[Aau's Song]]" by [[Triggerfish Animation Studios]] reflect its creators' love for South Africa's natural landscapes despite the myriad issues "stemming from our recent history" and South Africans' wide range of backgrounds.<ref name="South Africa">{{SW|url=news/star-wars-visions-aaus-song-interview|text=Meet the Visionaries: Nadia Darries and Daniel Clarke on the Celebration of Nature and Family in "Aau's Song"}}</ref>

====Culture war: social media and election fears====
{{Quote|It's not lost on me how, the way that these events have unfolded, is also due to the hyper divisiveness of the time that we live in&mdash;that is driven, I would say by this point, by echo chambers of thought and algorithms that reinforce our biases, and I think that applies to everybody.|Amandla Stenberg, in a video discussing the hateful harassment that she has received in 2024|<ref name="Stenberg statement">{{YouTube|channel=@JediNewsNetwork|name=Jedi News|video=41VtK78_J4c|text=Amandla Stenberg on Star Wars: The Acolyte Cancellation - Full Instagram Story Video - August 2024|date=2024-08-29}} &mdash; Reposted from [[Amandla Stenberg]]'s [[Wikipedia:Instagram|Instagram]] stories, posted on [[August 28]], [[2024]]</ref>}}
=====Endemic fandom drama=====
[[File:Twitter_icon.png|thumb|right|120px|Social media platforms such as Twitter have become battlegrounds of political discontent and division.]]
Lucasfilm employees and the leads of the sequel trilogy have faced considerable harassment over the years, particularly sexist and racist messages and death threats online. Social media escalated toxic antagonism among ''Star Wars'' communities and have become an avenue for mass outrage influenced by Russian troll accounts.<ref name="Russian trolls" /> The author [[Chuck Wendig]] faced years of harassment following the publication of his novel [[Aftermath (novel)|''Aftermath'']] in 2015 on including LGBT characters and was dropped by [[Marvel Comics]] from his ''Star Wars'' work in October 2018 for his outspoken political comments on social media.<ref name="Wendig blog">{{WebCite|url=https://terribleminds.com/ramble/2018/10/12/in-which-i-am-fired-from-marvel|text=In Which I Am Fired From Marvel|author=[[Chuck Wendig]]|work=TerribleMinds.com|archivedate=20250312081505}}</ref> This followed an upsurge in an online alt-right mob of the [[Wikipedia:Comicsgate|Comicsgate]] movement targeting Wendig on [[Wikipedia:YouTube|YouTube]] and Twitter that was joined by Twitter bot accounts.<ref name="Wendig Vox">{{WebCite|url=https://www.vox.com/culture/2018/10/24/17995502/twitter-trolls-bots-chuck-wendig-bethany-lacina|text=The new troll: how bots and puppets make internet outrage seem louder than it is|author=Aja Romano|date=2018-10-24|work=[[Wikipedia:Vox (website)|''Vox'']]|archivedate=20181025002303}}</ref>

Lucasfilm made social media posts commemorating [[Wikipedia:Martin Luther King Jr. Day|Martin Luther King Jr. Day]] on [[January 18]], 2021, quoting: "''There comes a time when one must take a position that is neither safe, nor politic, nor popular, but he must take it because conscience tells him it is right.''"<ref name="MLK2021">{{Facebook|username=lucasfilm|url=posts/there-comes-a-time-when-one-must-take-a-position-that-is-neither-safe-nor-politi/3878406025550734|name=[[Lucasfilm]]|date=2021-01-18|quote=“There comes a time when one must take a position that is neither safe, nor politic, nor popular, but he must take it because conscience tells him it is right.” - Martin Luther King, Jr.<br />Today, we honor Dr. King and celebrate his legacy. The Lucasfilm family is committed to the fight for justice and equality in every corner of the galaxy. Each of us keeps the dream alive!<br />(Photo by Reg Lancaster/Express/Getty Images)|archivedate=20251216224501}}</ref> Lucasfilm issued a statement on [[February 10]] that year, concerning ''The Mandalorian'' actress [[Gina Carano]], saying that "her social media posts denigrating people based on their cultural and religious identities are abhorrent and unacceptable" following months of online backlash against her politically charged posts, culminating in one that was widely reported as [[Wikipedia:Antisemitism|anti-Semitic]].<ref name="Carano">{{WebCite|url=https://io9.gizmodo.com/gina-carano-is-no-longer-a-part-of-star-wars-1846244896|text=Gina Carano Is No Longer a Part of ''Star Wars''|author=Lussier, Germain|date=2021-02-10|work=Gizmodo|archivedate=20210211025403}}</ref> When [[Moses Ingram]] shared her thoughts on receiving hateful messages about her role in the ''Obi-Wan Kenobi'' series on [[Wikipedia:Instagram|Instagram]] on [[May 31]], 2022, she thanked the fans who supported her in light of the abuse. Later that day, the official ''Star Wars'' Twitter account shared [[Ewan McGregor]]'s video message in solidarity with his co-star, saying that "if you're sending her bullying messages, you're no ''Star Wars'' fan." The ''Star Wars'' account made a further statement condemning the abuse, including "If anyone intends to make her feel in any way unwelcome, we have only one thing to say: we resist" and adding that "There are more than 20 million sentient species in the Star Wars galaxy, don't choose to be a racist."<ref name="Ingram">{{WebCite|url=https://www.theguardian.com/tv-and-radio/2022/jun/01/disney-and-ewan-mcgregor-condemn-horrendous-racism-sent-to-obi-wan-kenobi-star-moses-ingram|text='Star Wars' calls out racism after attacks on Moses Ingram|author=Cain, Sian|date=2022-06-01|work=[[Wikipedia:The Guardian|''The Guardian'']]|archivedate=20220603155230}}</ref>

=====''The Acolyte''=====
{{Main|Star Wars: The Acolyte#Release and reception}}
[[File:AmandlaStenbergViolin.jpg|thumb|left|300px|Amandla Stenberg playing the violin solo that John Williams had written for her.]]
[[Leslye Headland]]'s 2024 television series ''[[Star Wars: The Acolyte]]'' is set during the end of the High Republic Era, and Headland compared the state of the Republic to the [[Wikipedia:United States non-interventionism|isolationist tendencies]] of the United States [[Wikipedia:Interwar period|between the World Wars]].<ref name="Vanity Headland">{{WebCite|url=https://www.vanityfair.com/hollywood/2022/05/the-acolyte-star-wars-series|text=''The Acolyte'': How ''Star Wars'' Is About to Venture Back in Time|author=Anthony Breznican|date=2022-05-22|work=[[Wikipedia:Vanity Fair (magazine)|''Vanity Fair'']]|archivedate=20220524163919}}</ref> Concept illustration of the Galactic Senate Rotunda being built was made for the series, symbolizing the beginning of the corruption that would destroy the Republic in the prequel trilogy.<ref name="Art of Acolyte">''[[The Art of Star Wars: The Acolyte]]''</ref>

[[Amandla Stenberg]], who played [[Verosha Aniseya|Osha]] and [[Mae-ho Aniseya|Mae Aniseya]] in the series, made a music video addressing her exhaustion with targeted online discourse and harassment. She criticized the transformation of the term "[[Wikipedia:Woke|woke]]" for [[Wikipedia:Culture war|culture war]] and [[Wikipedia:Clickbait|clickbait]] purposes, including journalists who sacrifice "truth" for selective "metrics and data" that fuel division.<ref name="Stenberg MV">{{Instagram|username=amandlastenberg|url=reel/C8aorYEx2ZQ|name=[[Amandla Stenberg]]|date=2024-06-19|quote=Happy Juneteenth 🖤 and to those who are flooding me with intolerable racism—since it took me 72 hours on my laptop to make this song and video, u got 72 hours to respond. and I expect choreo!!|archivedate=20240620233032}}</ref> The cast and crew of ''The Acolyte'' faced racist and sexist online harassment. On [[August 19]], Lucasfilm and Disney informed media outlets that there would not be a second season of ''The Acolyte'' and offered no further comment. Twitter owner [[Wikipedia:Elon Musk|Elon Musk]] later reacted to the news on the social media platform to suggest that the series is "woke."<ref name="Star Wars fans">{{WebCite|url=https://www.rollingstone.com/tv-movies/tv-movie-features/acolyte-canceled-star-wars-fandom-1235086713|text='Acolyte' Canceled: Can Best of Star Wars Survive Worst of Its Fans?|author=CT Jones|date=2024-08-23|work=[[Wikipedia:Rolling Stone|Rolling Stone]]|archivedate=20241002133056}}</ref> Stenberg later shared their thoughts on Instagram, calling out the "hyper-conservative bigotry and vitriol, prejudice, hatred, and hateful language" used by "the alt-right" to target individuals, with "echo chambers of thought and algorithms that reinforce our biases" driving the "hyper divisiveness" of online discourse. Stenberg also said "[[Wikipedia:You think you just fell out of a coconut tree?|we all exist in the context in all which we live]]," calling on people to vote in the [[Wikipedia:2024 United States elections|2024 United States elections]] and to "challenge us all and challenge myself to continue questioning what it is that I digest and think critically about what shapes it."<ref name="Stenberg statement" />

===="You take a negative and you make it into a positive"====
[[File:Megalopolis.png|thumb|right|360px|Francis Ford Coppola's ''Megalopolis'' compares American democracy to the fall of the Roman Republic.]]
Responding to a question about "white men" dominating ''Star Wars'' at 2024's Cannes Film Festival, George Lucas said that most beings in his universe were aliens and his films starred prominent non-white cast members and showed strong women. Lucas further said he intended his universe to show that all people are "equal" regardless of their appearance, gender, or species, with the only form of discrimination being anti-droid sentiment similar to the contemporary development of [[Wikipedia:Artificial general intelligence|advanced artificial intelligence]].<ref name="Cannes 2024" /> Lucas had said in 2020 that the [[D-Squad]] arc on existentialism in droids were his favourite episodes of ''The Clone Wars''.<ref name="SWS 20200430">{{StarWarsShow|4NgPKSzR0LU|George Lucas and Dave Filoni Talk The Clone Wars, Plus Anthony Daniels Stops By!|sw_url=video/george-lucas-and-dave-filoni-talk-the-clone-wars-plus-anthony-daniels-stops-by}}</ref> The [[Wikipedia:English people|English]] comic writer [[Alan Moore]] had similarly written "[[Rust Never Sleeps]]," among several other comics in ''Star Wars'' Legends published in [[1982]], on the idea of artificial intelligence's having no rights compared to organic beings. Moore noted in 2024 that British and American culture and politics have, over the decades of the Internet's democratization, become dominated by the adverse effect of fandoms&mdash;a sense of ignorance, entitlement, frustration, and complaint where "entertainments may be cancelled prematurely through an adverse fan reaction," "misogynist crusades such as Gamergate or Comicsgate," and elections are treated as a fandom event&mdash;which Moore believed has been fueling superficial politics with dangerous effects on the [[Wikipedia:Right-wing populism|populist]] road to fascism.<ref name="Moore">{{WebCite|url=https://www.theguardian.com/books/2024/oct/26/fandom-has-toxified-the-world-watchmen-author-alan-moore-on-superheroes-comicsgate-and-trump|text=‘Fandom has toxified the world’: Watchmen author Alan Moore on superheroes, Comicsgate and Trump|author=[[Alan Moore]]|date=2024-10-26|work=[[Wikipedia:The Guardian|''The Guardian'']]|archivedate=20241101211529}}</ref>

Lucas' filmmaker friend Francis Ford Coppola's film [[Wikipedia:Megalopolis|''Megalopolis'']] premiered at the 2024 Cannes festival, exploring the similar premise that the United States of America is a new Roman Republic and, like Rome two thousand years ago, coud lose its republic and democracy<ref name="Megalopolis May">{{WebCite|url=https://www.theguardian.com/film/article/2024/may/17/francis-ford-coppola-us-politics-republic-rome-megalopolis-cannes|text=Francis Ford Coppola: US politics is at 'the point where we might lose our republic'|author=Philip Oltermann|date=2024-05-17|work=[[Wikipedia:The Guardian|''The Guardian'']]|archivedate=20240530120859}}</ref> at the [[Wikipedia:2024 United States presidential election|2024 US election]]. Coppola mentioned Lucas as an influence and mourned that journalism and Hollywood industries are "dying," and America's existing "system" of consumerism has been making an unhappy people. With ''Megalopolis'', he wanted to highlight that humanity is an "extraordinary" civilization of which every person has a creative power, even given their circumstances, to change themselves and their surrounding world for humanity to mutually thrive.<ref name="Megalopolis September">{{WebCite|url=https://www.npr.org/2024/09/27/nx-s1-4964777/megalopolis-francis-ford-coppola-adam-driver-aubrey-plaza|text=Coppola's 'Megalopolis' sees the fall of the Roman Republic as a cautionary tale|author=Mandalit del Barco|date=2024-09-28|work=[[Wikipedia:NPR|NPR]]|archivedate=20240930211217}}</ref>

[[File:Empire-RollingStones.jpg|thumb|left|300px|Billy Dee Williams, Mark Hamill, Carrie Fisher, and Harrison Ford on the ''Rolling Stone'' magazine, 1980, photographed by Annie Leibovitz.]]
Lando Calrissian actor [[Billy Dee Williams]], who had lived through America's [[Wikipedia:Jim Crow laws|Jim Crow]] era of segregation and been friends with civil rights figure [[Wikipedia:James Baldwin|James Baldwin]],<ref name="BDW Guardian">{{WebCite|url=https://www.theguardian.com/culture/2024/feb/21/billy-dee-williams-memoir-star-wars|text=Billy Dee Williams: 'At this stage in my life, I don't need to apologise for anything'|author=David Smith|date=2024-02-21|work=[[Wikipedia:The Guardian|''The Guardian'']]|archivedate=20240222023228}}</ref> was initially concerned about the racialist impression that the "black fear" of Darth Vader, voiced by James Earl Jones, could give in contrast to the "white knight" of [[Alec Guinness]]' Obi-Wan Kenobi, but following 1980's release of ''The Empire Strikes Back'', he was pleased that ''Star Wars'' has a clearer symbolism of humanity's darkness as the antithesis of clarity. Drawing on his conversations with director [[Irvin Kershner]] on Japanese philosophy, Williams described ''Star Wars'' as a lesson in truths and consequences about abuse of power, and that the "greatest teacher" is the one who says "Don't follow me, follow yourself. Because within you there is that kingdom, that life, that force."<ref name="Rolling Stone 1980">{{WebCite|url=https://www.rollingstone.com/culture/culture-news/star-wars-slaves-to-the-empire-61931|text='Star Wars': Slaves to the 'Empire'|author=Timothy White|work=[[Wikipedia:Rolling Stone|''Rolling Stone'']]|archivedate=20190519150233}}</ref> Williams had been aware that his younger sequel trilogy leads faced racism and sexism from online trolls by 2019 and,<ref name="Esquire">{{WebCite|url=https://www.esquire.com/entertainment/movies/a29817501/billy-dee-williams-star-wars-the-rise-of-skywalker-lando-calrissian-interview|text=The Enduring, Intergalactic Cool of Billy Dee Williams|author=Matt Miller|date=2019-11-26|work=[[Wikipedia:Esquire (magazine)|''Esquire'']]|archivedate=20191126215223}}</ref> in an interview promoting his memoir in 2024, said he believes that while the world has been dominated by "a European western value system for centuries," ultimately he does not care about discussing identity politics, saying: "The only thing that's dangerous is lots of stupid people running around and shooting people," and that "you take a negative and you make it into a positive."<ref name="BDW Guardian" />

Dr. [[Chris Kempshall]]'s ''[[Star Wars: The Rise and Fall of the Galactic Empire]]'' was first published in the United Kingdom on [[July 4]], 2024, the day of the [[Wikipedia:2024 United Kingdom general election|UK general election]], as an analysis of the Galactic Empire from an in-universe historical perspective.<ref name="DKUK">{{DK|url=uk/book/9780241655047-star-wars-the-rise-and-fall-of-the-galactic-empire|text=Star Wars The Rise and Fall of the Galactic Empire}}</ref> It would complement the ''[[Star Wars: Reign of the Empire]]'' novel series.<ref name="Rise Inspired">{{Twitter|DarthInternous|status/1817952147222282346|[[Tom Hoeler]]|quote=I may or may not have sent Rebecca some notes based on tidbits in your book that said "ooo let's explore this" {{C|In response to: "I'm choosing to believe that there was just a photo of me on all of your desks for this and the 'do it for him' Simpsons message."}}|archiveurl=https://archive.ph/WoXmD}}</ref> On [[November 5]], the day of the 2024 US elections, Industrial Light & Magic encouraged Americans to "Vote and make your voice heard" via Twitter, including the image previously posted by the official ''Star Wars'' account ahead of the US elections in 2020.<ref name="2024 election">{{Twitter|ILMVFX|status/1853841579066392818|[[Industrial Light & Magic]]|archiveurl=https://archive.is/i5cIs}}</ref>

====Inspiration and insight in Latin America====
{{Quote|This is a story that gives me so much fuel to keep going forward, because when I get to bring characters like this to life and they come from me and what that represents it, I hope that it serves as a source of inspiration, representation and for the Venezuelan people to see that it is possible and that it doesn't always have to end in demise and pain.|Humberly González, in an interview on ''Star Wars Outlaws'' and the Venezuelan people's fight for democracy in 2024|<ref name="SI interview">{{WebCite|url=https://www.si.com/videogames/features/star-wars-outlaws-interview-humberly-gonzalez|text=Star Wars Outlaws star on fighting for role as franchise's first Venezuelan-born hero|author=Griff Griffin|date=2024-08-22|work=[[Wikipedia:Sports Illustrated|''Sports Illustrated'']]|archivedate=20241004172515}}</ref>}}
The original actor of Chewbacca, [[Peter Mayhew]], set up a fundraising campaign with his foundation in May 2018 for a charity group based in [[Wikipedia:Texas|Texas]], USA, supplying food and other daily essentials to children in Venezuela. The campaign was supported by members of the 501st Legion fan group in Texas, and Mayhew was moved by the deteriorating conditions in Venezuela after meeting Elisa Argüello, a [[Wikipedia:Venezuelans|Venezuelan-American]] neighbor and ''Star Wars'' fan, following the [[Wikipedia:2018 Venezuelan presidential election|sham election]] that month confirming [[Wikipedia:Nicolás Maduro|Nicolás Maduro's]] continued leadership of the authoritarian [[Wikipedia:Chavismo|Chavismo]] regime.<ref name="Mayhew Venezuela">{{WebCite|url=https://www.pressherald.com/2018/06/11/chewbacca-actor-stands-up-for-poor-in-venezuela-with-fundraising-campaign|text=Chewbacca actor stands up for poor in Venezuela with fundraising campaign|date=2018-06-11|work=[[Wikipedia:Portland Press Herald|''Portland Press Herald'']]|archivedate=20180612121400}}</ref>

The leading woman of the 2024 video game ''[[Star Wars Outlaws]]'' is the Venezuelan-Canadian actress [[Humberly González]]. She expressed her belief that her character [[Kay Vess]]' resilience and struggles reflect the experiences of her people, who had again [[Wikipedia:2024 Venezuelan presidential election|voted for a democratic future]] in June of that year but the same regime was somehow "still here 22 years later"&mdash;despite the world feeling "rigged" against people, Vess' story of not giving up serves as an inspiration because "freedom is worth fighting for." González also thought that her separation from her family who made sacrifices for her to emigrate from their impoverished and oppressive homeland echoes Vess' story, coupled with her immigrant experience of learning to open up and trust people in the liberal democracy of Canada to pursue her dream career as an actress.<ref name="SI interview" />

Cassian Andor actor and ''Andor'' executive producer [[Diego Luna]] wrote an essay published by [[Wikipedia:The New York Times|''The New York Times'']] on [[December 4]], 2025, stating that the creators of ''Andor'' aimed to reflect on the past and examine citizens who, having given power to "would-be dictators," would choose to rise up against authoritarianism. He placed the series in the context of the contemporary social and political climate of the [[Wikipedia:Western Hemisphere|Western Hemisphere]], dominated by the "reality TV" of sensationalist leaders such as Donald Trump of the United States, Nicolás Maduro of Venezuela, [[Wikipedia:Javier Milei|Javier Milei]] of Argentina, [[Wikipedia:Jair Bolsonaro|Jair Bolsonaro]] of Brazil, [[Wikipedia:Nayib Bukele|Nayib Bukele]] of [[Wikipedia:El Salvador|El Salvador]], [[Wikipedia:Daniel Ortega|Daniel Ortega]] and [[Wikipedia:Rosario Murillo|Rosario Murillo]] of [[Wikipedia:Nicaragua|Nicaragua]], and [[Wikipedia:Andrés Manuel López Obrador|Andrés Manuel López Obrador]] of [[Wikipedia:Mexico|Mexico]]. However, Luna wrote, the audience have remarkably used ''Andor'' to discuss their present political concerns before the creators spoke about them, and the role of fiction in questioning reality leads to the question: "Why do we need fiction as a mediator in order to help us understand certain realities?" Luna thought that fully engaging in a story is not only escapism but "an opportunity for transformation" as the experience could give the audience insight into their own selves.<ref name="Luna NYT">{{WebCite|url=https://www.nytimes.com/2025/12/04/special-series/diego-luna-andor-fiction-media.html|text=Diego Luna: Our Reality Has Become Reality TV|author=[[Diego Luna]]|date=2025-12-04|work=[[Wikipedia:The New York Times|''The New York Times'']]|archiveurl=https://ghostarchive.org/archive/zPDiR}}</ref>

====''The Revolution Starts Now''====
=====Love in the time of unrest=====
{{Quote|No one goes in a revolution if it's not for love, for a real need of bringing change for a community. ''[&hellip;]'' It's a love story. There's no revolution without love.|Diego Luna, in April 2025, on ''Andor''|<ref name="Luna love">{{YouTube|channel=heyuguysblog|name=HeyUGuys|video=a_KQmQpalgY|text=Andor Season 2 - Revolution Needs Passion: Love Fuels Change|date=2025-04-11}}</ref>}}
[[File:AndorSeason2-Poster.jpg|thumb|right|260px|In 2025, ''Andor'' and ''The Mask of Fear'' explore the start of ''Star Wars''{{'}} revolution.]]
According to [[Alexander Freed]], his February 2025 novel ''[[Reign of the Empire: The Mask of Fear]]'' explores the "violent disagreement" of "'ordinary' citizens" in the Empire's early days on the "ideas of democracy, resistance, revolution, and complicity."<ref name="Freed Gizmodo">{{WebCite|url=https://gizmodo.com/star-wars-reign-of-the-empire-books-alexander-freed-andor-2000480472|text=''Star Wars''{{'}} New Book Trilogy Explores the Politics Behind the Rise of the Rebellion|date=2024-07-29|work=Gizmodo|archivedate=20240729152141}}</ref> Editor [[Tom Hoeler]] further said that the book was informed by political movements in the real world and developed with the same intent as the television series ''Andor''<ref name="MoF Tom">{{Substack|subdomain=thecubicleattheendofthegalaxy|url=p/star-wars-reign-of-the-empire-the|text=Star Wars Reign of the Empire: The Mask of Fear - 100 Things|name=The Cubicle at the End of the Galaxy|author=[[Tom Hoeler]]|date=2025-02-11}}</ref>&mdash;according to Freed, his novel and ''Andor'' are "both interested in what it means to live under an authoritarian regime, the psychological harm that builds up under those conditions, and the sacrifices people make."<ref name="MOF geek">{{WebCite|url=https://templeofgeek.com/qa-with-alexander-freed-star-wars-the-mask-of-fear|text=Q&A With Alexander Freed &#124; “Star Wars: The Mask of Fear”|author=Danielle Schwertner|date=2025-02-18|work=Temple of Geek|archivedate=20250401010026}}</ref> Freed also said that all ''Star Wars'' work involve a clear moral world of good and evil based on the Force&mdash;even while characters allow a depth of nuance to be explored&mdash;the notion of a found family, whether literal or forging a sense of community with likeminded people&mdash;and faith in peace and being able to discuss differences while still accepting that negotiations often descend into violence.<ref name="MOF SWNN">{{YouTube|video=HV2x7EThMx4|text=An Interview With Alexander Freed - Author of Reign of the Empire: The Mask of Fear|channel=@StarWarsNewsNetVideos|name=Star Wars News Net|date=2025-02-25}}</ref> ''Andor'' Season 2, released in April and May that year, had its trailer released in February using [[Wikipedia:Steve Earle|Steve Earle's]] 2004 [[Wikipedia:Cowpunk|country punk]] song [[Wikipedia:The Revolution Starts Now (album)|''The Revolution Starts Now'']],<ref name="Andor 2 trailer">{{SWYouTube|AE4wxt70aUM|Andor &#124; Season 2 Trailer &#124; Streaming April 22 on Disney+}}</ref> which addresses how citizens ought to actively defend American democracy in light of the election and ongoing war in Iraq that year.<ref name="Earle">Liner notes of [[Wikipedia:The Revolution Starts Now (album)|''The Revolution Starts Now'']] &mdash; {{WebCite|url=https://www.steveearle.net/discography/revolution.php|text=The Revolution Starts&hellip;Now|work=[https://www.steveearle.net SteveEarle.net]|archivedate=20241201170414}}</ref>

Diego Luna said in an interview in [[April 11]], 2025 that revolutions are based on love.<ref name="Luna love" /> [[Elizabeth Dulau]], who played Kleya Marki, said in an interview published on [[April 28]] that beneath the rigid veneer of her character was "a lot of love" that motivated her to sacrifice her daily life for the rebellion.<ref name="Dulau Vogue">{{WebCite|url=https://vogue.sg/elizabeth-dulau-kleya-marki-andor-season-2|text=“The rebellion is her entire life”: Elizabeth Dulau on playing Kleya Marki in ‘Andor’ Season 2|author=Azrin Tan|date=2025-04-28|work=[[Wikipedia:Vogue Singapore|''Vogue Singapore'']]|archivedate=20250428122249}}</ref> In an interview with [[Wikipedia:The Hollywood Reporter|''The Hollywood Reporter'']] published on April 15, showrunner Tony Gilroy credited Lucasfilm's President Kathleen Kennedy and Disney's CEO Bob Iger&mdash;who returned to the role in November 2022&mdash;with the "gamble" of backing ''Andor''{{'s}} story of political revolution even though streaming services like Disney+ were beginning to face market difficulties in mid-2022, and ''Andor'' was restructured from five season to two seasons rather than abandoned.<ref name="Gilroy April 15 THR">{{WebCite|url=https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/tv/tv-features/andor-creator-season-2-episodes-1236190457|text=Andor Season 2: Tony Gilroy Unpacks the Pressures of New Episodes|author=Brian Davids|date=2025-04-15|work=[[Wikipedia:The Hollywood Reporter|''The Hollywood Reporter'']]|archivedate=20250419021121}}</ref> In an interview with [[Wikipedia:The Daily Telegraph|''The Telegraph'']] published on [[April 23]], Tony Gilroy was asked if there was any order from Lucasfilm to "adjust the depiction of the Empire in light of a presidency that could be seen to mirror it" in light of Donald Trump's [[Wikipedia:Second presidency of Donald Trump|second term]] as US President. Gilroy replied that he would be asking the same question if he was in the interviewer's place but elaborated that his story and characters were based on historical revolutions&mdash;such as the Soviet revolutionaries [[Wikipedia:Leon Trotsky|Leon Trotsky]] and Stalin who [[Wikipedia:1907 Tiflis bank robbery|robbed a bank]] to fund the revolution,<ref name="Andor Telegraph">{{WebCite|url=https://www.telegraph.co.uk/tv/0/tony-gilroy-interview-andor-star-wars-disney-plus|text=Andor creator Tony Gilroy: ‘My Star Wars characters are based more on Trotsky than Trump’|author=Stephen Armstrong|date=2025-04-23|work=[[Wikipedia:The Daily Telegraph|''The Telegraph'']]|archivedate=20250423093955}}</ref> which was the basis for the Aldhani heist in Season 1,<ref name="Watch Gilroy roundtable">{{YouTube|video=yMFoXfbyjow|text=Andor Creator Tony Gilroy &#124; Roundtable Interview at Lucasfilm|channel=@childrenofthewatch|name=Children of the Watch: A Star Wars Show|date=2025-06-12}}</ref> or the French Resistance comprising [[Wikipedia:Charles de Gaulle|Gaullists]], Communists, and the [[Wikipedia:Maquis (World War II)|Maquis]] who hated each other.<ref name="Andor Telegraph" />

In a [[Wikipedia:Golden Globe Awards|Golden Globe]] press Q&A held on April 15 and published on [[April 20]], Tony Gilroy said he was informed by the revolutions of Haiti, Russia, America, and Britain, as well as early Catholicism and the Mexican revolutionary [[Wikipedia:Emiliano Zapata|Emiliano Zapata]].<ref name="Gilroy April 15">{{WebCite|url=https://www.thebullseye.no/p/andor-q-and-a-tony-gilroy|text='Andor' Q&A: Tony Gilroy|date=2025-04-20|work=''The Bulls Eye'' newsletter|archivedate=20250518042024}}</ref> In an interview with [[Wikipedia:Agence France-Presse|Agence France-Presse]] (AFP) in Paris published via [[Wikipedia:France 24|France 24]] on April 20, Gilroy added that he was also informed by the French Revolution, the English-born American and French revolutionary [[Wikipedia:Thomas Paine|Thomas Paine]], [[Wikipedia:Oliver Cromwell|Oliver Cromwell]], and the Roman Revolution. Gilroy also said to AFP that his writing of Ghorman was partly inspired by the 2009&ndash;2017 television series [[Wikipedia:Un village français|''A French Village'']], about Nazi-occupied France, and that "the dynamic of oppression and resistance are a [[Wikipedia:Breaking wheel|Catherine wheel]]" and history is "timeless, sadly."<ref name="Gilroy France24">{{WebCite|url=https://www.france24.com/en/live-news/20250420-star-wars-series-andor-back-for-final-season|text=Star Wars series 'Andor' back for final season|author=[[Wikipedia:Agence France-Presse|Agence France-Presse]]|date=2025-04-20|work=[[Wikipedia:France 24|France 24]]|archivedate=20250420055846}}</ref> Gilroy also said he was interested in Mao Zedong in another interview published that day, by [[Wikipedia:Time Out (magazine)|''Time Out'']], and he also took inspiration from the 1969 [[Wikipedia:Jean-Pierre Melville|Jean-Pierre Melville]] film [[Wikipedia:Army of Shadows|''Army of Shadows'']], set during the French Resistance, the 1982 [[Wikipedia:Jacques Demy|Jacques Demy]] film [[Wikipedia:A Room in Town|''A Room in Town'']], set in a 1955 strike in France, and Gillo Pontecorvo's 1966 film ''The Battle of Algiers'', with the writer also finding the 1943 [[Wikipedia:Jean Renoir|Jean Renoir]] film [[Wikipedia:This Land Is Mine (film)|''This Land Is Mine'']] echoing ''Andor''. The creators were also influenced by the television series [[Wikipedia:Babylon Berlin|''Babylon Berlin'']], set in the latter years of Weimar Germany.<ref name="Time Out Gilroy">{{WebCite|url=https://www.timeout.com/news/interview-star-wars-showrunner-tony-gilroy-on-andor-season-2-042025|text=Interview: Star Wars showrunner Tony Gilroy on ‘Andor’ season 2|author=Tom Huddleston|date=2025-04-20|work=[[Wikipedia:Time Out (magazine)|''Time Out'']]|archivedate=20250424135003}}</ref>

In an interview conducted ahead of Season 2's premiere and published upon the series finale's release on [[May 13]], [[Wikipedia:Variety (magazine)|''Variety'']] asked Tony Gilroy about motifs of unrest across ''Andor'', noting how the series "summons images of Ukraine, Gaza, Kabul and Hong Kong" of current affairs together with historical references&mdash;Gilroy said that he was writing about all revolutions and insurrections and how he felt that people, including himself, often have a narcissistic and individualistic belief that they are living in a unique time of history despite that not being "the pattern of history." Nevertheless, seeing audiences draw current real-world parallels to the series, such as a sense of authoritarianism threatening democracies like the United States, is a reflection on "if people find history knocking at their door, there's probably all kinds of places they can look for references."<ref name="Gilroy-Luna Variety">{{WebCite|url=https://variety.com/2025/tv/news/andor-finale-diego-luna-tony-gilroy-star-wars-1236394652|text=Andor Finale: Diego Luna, Tony Gilroy on Revolutionizing 'Star Wars'|author=Adam B. Vary|date=2025-05-13|work=[[Wikipedia:Variety (magazine)|''Variety'']]|archivedate=20250513161511}}</ref> The writer said in a roundtable discussion hosted by Lucasfilm and released on [[June 12]] that the "[[Wikipedia:English Revolution|British Revolution]]"&mdash;the [[Wikipedia:Wars of the Three Kingdoms|civil wars]] in the [[Wikipedia:British Isles|isles]] ruled intermittently by [[Wikipedia:Charles I of England|Charles I]], Oliver Cromwell, and [[Wikipedia:Charles II of England|Charles II]] during much of the 17th century and culminating in the [[Wikipedia:Glorious Revolution|Revolution of 1688]]&mdash;was the "ultimate revolution," as [[Wikipedia:Karl Marx|Karl Marx]] and [[Wikipedia:Friedrich Engels|Friedrich Engels]] had claimed.<ref name="Watch Gilroy roundtable" />

[[Stellan Skarsgård]], the actor of Luthen Rael in ''Andor'', specifically asked that his character not be motivated by the trope of revenge against the Empire. Tony Gilroy agreed and modeled Luthen Rael as an extreme [[Wikipedia:Accelerationism|accelerationist]] political revolutionary like Mao Zedong who incites desperate scenarios to compel people to revolt. Rael also takes inspiration from revolutionaries dedicated to connecting people and gathering resources like [[Wikipedia:Benjamin Franklin|Benjamin Franklin]].<ref name="Slash Tony Gilroy">{{WebCite|url=https://www.slashfilm.com/1857713/andor-showrunner-tony-gilroy-most-controversial-storyline-explained|text=Andor Showrunner Tony Gilroy Addresses The Series' Most Controversial Storyline [Exclusive Interview]|author=Ben Pearson|date=2025-04-14|work=[[Wikipedia:/Film|''SlashFilm'']]|archivedate=20250515090022}}</ref> Gilroy also described the character as a start-up company founder<ref name="Themes" /> who has secured [[Wikipedia:Venture capital|venture capital funding]] and is seeking to go public with the revolution in his garage but is terrible at [[Wikipedia:Human resource management|human resource management]].<ref name="Stone Tony Gilroy">{{WebCite|url=https://www.rollingstone.com/tv-movies/tv-movie-features/andor-tony-gilroy-season-2-interview-darth-vader-1235324435|text=Andor Showrunner Tony Gilroy Season 2 Interview: Darth Vader and More|author=Brian Hiatt|date=2025-04-25|work=[[Wikipedia:Rolling Stone|''Rolling Stone'']]|archivedate=20250427094229}}</ref> As for Saw Gerrera, Gilroy said his character is an "anarchist" and also the only one with "clarity of purpose."<ref name="Reason Gilroy">{{WebCite|url=https://reason.com/podcast/2025/12/23/andor-creator-tony-gilroy-on-bureaucracy-and-the-surveillance-state|text=''Andor'' Creator Tony Gilroy on Bureaucracy and the Surveillance State|author=Eric Boehm|date=2025-12-23|work=[[Wikipedia:Reason Foundation|Reason Foundation]]|archivedate=20251228125114}}</ref>

[[Forest Whitaker]], played Saw Gerrera in ''Andor'' and ''Rogue One'' and the Ugandan dictator [[Wikipedia:Idi Amin|Idi Amin]] in the 2006 film [[Wikipedia:The Last King of Scotland (film)|''The Last King of Scotland'']], said he was drawn to both stories as they are about characters compelled to fight for a cause; Amin and Gerrera, "in their own ways," led rebellions that they believed would protect their people from being "subjugated by their environments." Whitaker did not base his potrayal of Gerrera on any direct historical research, though he was partly inspired by the [[Wikipedia:Latin America|Latin American]] revolutionary [[Wikipedia:Che Guevara|Che Guevara]] as a leader who "fought for his people from the outskirts of society." Whitaker added that he appreciated ''Andor'' for exploring the "gray areas" of "flawed leaders, misguided rebels, and figures who'd both torture and nurture" like Saw Gerrera, an exemplification of the violent pursuit of a cause "by whatever means necessary," yet audiences connect to the character because his "motivations are just."<ref name="REBELION">''[[REBELIÓN: An Andor Zine]]''</ref> Writer Beau Willimon described Saw Gerrera as being insane but pure in his righteous purpose, comparing him to [[Wikipedia:Robert Duvall|Robert Duvall's]] character in ''Apocalypse Now'' announcing "I love the smell of [[Wikipedia:Napalm|napalm]] in the morning."<ref name="Willimon 4-6">{{YouTube|video=OJFS1lMNJfo|text=Andor S2 Q&A - Beau Willimon - Episodes 4-6|channel=@backstorymagazine2757|name=Backstory Magazine|date=2025-04-30}}</ref> Whitaker himself had founded the Whitaker Peace and Development Initiative in 2012, which has been developing programs to train and support marginalized people in places afflicted by conflict, with an emphasis on enabling youth and women to build more resilient communities working with their environment.<ref name="REBELION" />

Willimon emphasized that [[Cinta Kaz]] and Vel Sartha worked for the rebel cause together for the sake of their love for each other, in the vein of [[Wikipedia:John le Carré|John le Carré's]] novels. He recounted that Tony Gilroy said people like them pave the path for revolution not for the sake of personal glory, for which reason they are all glorious&mdash;even Cinta Kaz's death was glorious due to the way Vel Sartha characterized it, from a place of love.<ref name="Willimon 4-6" />

=====The weight of words=====
{{Quote|In the ''Star Wars'' universe, the Galactic Senate had a very long history as a governing body that was built on democratic principles, so through the five years of ''Andor'', which we've condensed into two years ''[seasons]'', we're watching many events, but we're watching the destruction and obliteration of democratic principles, and the Senate is descending&mdash;we're watching it through Mon's eyes over her shoulder&mdash;the Senate descending into total capitulation to the Emperor's rule.|Dan Gilroy|<ref name="Ankler">{{YouTube|video=Wyi0DanN6BU|text=Art & Crafts: Making of ‘Andor’ Season 2|channel=@The-Ankler|name=The Ankler|date=2025-08-25}}</ref>}}
[[File:PalmoPlazaVFX.jpg|thumb|right|260px|The set of Palmo Plaza (above) and the finalized shot (below).]]
Tony Gilroy was permitted to use the word "fascism" as part of ''Andor''{{'s}} press tour towards the end of the series' release on Disney+.<ref name="ATX Andor">{{YouTube|video=FMOw6gP4r7Q|text=ANDOR with Tony Gilroy & Beau Willimon &#124; ATX TV Festival|channel=@ATXTV|name=ATX TV|date=2025-06-10}}</ref> On May 13, Gilroy said during a livestream on the official ''Star Wars'' YouTube channel that fascism not only destroys the people that the ideology is used to target but also the people who enact it, referring to ISB supervisor Dedra Meero and the bureaucrat Syril Karn.<ref name="Finale livestream">{{SWYouTube|uvkpcedAtXs|Andor Season 2 with Diego Luna and Tony Gilroy LIVE &#124; Now Streaming on Disney+}}</ref> The ISB features prominently in the series and, in Gilroy's words, "isn't made up of caricatures" but people who believe in the "righteousness of control," with mundane bureaucracy becoming an oppressive weapon that destroys even its own enforcers.<ref name="REBELION" /> The ISB originated in the ''Star Wars'' Legends continuity in [[1987]]'s [[Star Wars: The Roleplaying Game (first edition)|''Star Wars: The Roleplaying Game'']] by [[West End Games]],<ref name="Rebel Breakout">{{WEGCite|story=Rebel Breakout|book=Star Wars: The Roleplaying Game (first edition)}}</ref> created by [[Bill Slavicsek]] as the Empire's "[[Wikipedia:KGB|KGB]] or secret police." For 1990's ''The Rebel Alliance Sourcebook'', Slavicsek signed off on Paul Murphy's creation of the Ghorman Massacre, which drew inspiration from the [[Wikipedia:British Indian Army|British Indian Army's]] [[Wikipedia:Jallianwala Bagh massacre|Jallianwala Bagh massacre]] in [[1910s|1919]] based on his reading of historian [[Wikipedia:Jan Morris|Jan Morris’s]] [[Wikipedia:Pax Britannica Trilogy|''Pax Britannica Trilogy'']].<ref name="Paul Murphy">{{WebCite|url=https://www.theringer.com/2025/05/08/star-wars/andor-lore-star-wars-rpg-history-ghorman-massacre-paul-murphy-west-end-games|text=The Roots of ‘Andor’ Lore Run Deep|author=Lindbergh, Ben|date=2025-05-09|work=[[Wikipedia:The Ringer (website)|The Ringer]]|archivedate=20250609215800}}</ref>

Tony Gilroy placed emphasis on a non-ideological study of characters, like the Dedra Meero as an [[Imperial Kinder-block]] victim trying to "become the thing that scares me ''[her]'' the most" and Syril Karn's "romantic" [[Wikipedia:Careerism|careerism]] and "small-mindedness" in greed, explicitly drawing on Hannah Arendt's banality of evil and events such as the Nazi [[Wikipedia:Wannsee Conference|Wannsee Conference]] for Krennic's conference on the genocide of Ghorman but without making his writing an academic study, all the while personally struggling with understanding why people do things they know to be wrong.<ref name="Charlie Sykes Gilroy">{{YouTube|video=kryBbSu5wE4|text=Tony Gilroy: Yes, ‘Andor’ Rhymes with Our Political Moment|channel=@charlessykes9046|name=To the Contrary With Charlie Sykes|date=2025-08-12}}</ref> The showrunner also cited the 2001 [[Wikipedia:Frank Pierson|Frank Pierson]] film [[Wikipedia:Conspiracy (2001 film)|''Conspiracy'']], which dramatizes the Wannsee Conference, as an influence for the Ghorman operation.<ref name="Gilroy April 15 THR" /> According to [[Dan Gilroy]], brother of Tony Gilroy and the writer of the episodes "[[Messenger]]," "[[Who Are You?]]" and "[[Welcome to the Rebellion]]," and the nominee for an [[Wikipedia:Emmy Awards|Emmy Award]] for the writing of the lattermost episode, the Ghorman Massacre serves as the "fulcrum" of the entire series and a radicalization event for both the Imperial and rebel sides&mdash;a being of the galaxy either buys into Krennic's fascistic narrative like Dedra Meero or otherwise chooses not to act and becomes a bureaucrat or some other part of the "bonfire of fascism" like Syril Karn, or they choose to act against the Empire before it is too late. Dan thought that Syril Karn's final moment is akin to Alec Guinness' portrayal of epiphany&mdash;"what have I done?"&mdash;at the end of the 1957 [[Wikipedia:David Lean|David Lean]] film [[Wikipedia:The Bridge on the River Kwai|''The Bridge on the River Kwai'']].<ref name="Ankler" />

[[File:BattleOfAlgiers.png|thumb|left|350px|''Andor'' drew inspiration from various film and events, including the 1966 film ''The Battle of Algiers'' on the Algerian Revolution.]]
[[Janus Metz]], the director of the same Season 2 arc and an Emmy Award nominee for the direction of "Who Are You?," which shows the Ghorman Massacre, had previously filmed [[Wikipedia:Danish Defence|Danish soldiers]] serving in [[Wikipedia:Helmand Province|Helmand]] in the Afghanistan War for the 2010 documentary [[Wikipedia:Armadillo (2010 film)|''Armadillo'']] and wanted to convey the sense of being amidst a real firefight.<ref name="REBELION" /> At the set of Palmo Plaza, Metz and the crew discussed films and events serving as inspiration, including Gillo Pontecorvo's ''The Battle of Algiers'' (1966), [[Wikipedia:Romain Gavras|Romain Gavras']] [[Wikipedia:Athena (2022 film)|''Athena'']] (2022), [[Wikipedia:Football hooliganism|football riots]], and street fights in the Gaza Strip.<ref name="Ankler" /> The episode was filmed "almost like a documentary" and intentionally grounded in the sense of control and resistance prevalent under authoritarian regimes during the Cold War and contemporary surveillance states. The director credited the all-French cast of actors who sang the Ghorman anthem "[[We Are the Ghor]]" by composer [[Nicholas Britell]] and Tony Gilroy for delivering a sense of belonging and resistance that is poignant not only in the ''Star Wars'' universe but also in the real world.<ref name="REBELION" /> The Ghorman protest phrase "we are the Ghor, the galaxy is watching" borrows from the real life protest phrase "[[Wikipedia:The whole world is watching|the whole world is watching]]," which is, according to Gilroy, "universal enough that I can use it," based on its use during the [[Wikipedia:Occupy Wall Street|Occupy Wall Street]] movement in [[2011]]. The phrase was popularized in the 1960s by civil rights protests, [[Wikipedia:Bob Dylan|Bob Dylan's]] "[[Wikipedia:The Times They Are a-Changin' (song)|The Times They Are a-Changin']]," and the [[Wikipedia:1968 Democratic National Convention protests|anti–Vietnam War protests]] in Chicago.<ref name="MashableInterview">{{WebCite|url=https://mashable.com/article/andor-galaxy-watching|text='The galaxy is watching!' How 'Andor' connects to a history of protest|author=Chris Taylor Lambie|date=2025-05-07|work=[[Wikipedia:Mashable|''Mashable'']]|archivedate=20250508050855}}</ref>

The role of singing in resistance movements such as among [[Wikipedia:Denmark in World War II|Danes under Nazi occupation]] and [[Wikipedia:Singing Revolution|Estonians under Soviet occupation]] inspired Tony Gilroy to make the Ghorman anthem, while Nazi false flag operations at the start of Germany and the Soviet Union's [[Wikipedia:Invasion of Poland|Invasion of Poland]] inspired Dan Gilroy's writing of the sniper at Palmo Plaza.<ref name="Gilroys Backstory">{{YouTube|video=dPLJESDwpU0|text=Andor S2 Emmys Q&A - Tony Gilroy - Dan Gilroy|channel=@backstorymagazine2757|name=Backstory Magazine|date=2025-08-24}}</ref> [[Wikipedia:Los Angeles Times|''Los Angeles Times''{{'}}]] Spanish language division asked Tony Gilroy on [[May 30]] if he was informed by the 1968 [[Wikipedia:Tlatelolco massacre|Tlatelolco massacre]] in [[Wikipedia:Mexico City|Mexico City]] or something similar like 1989's Tiananmen Square massacre, and Gilroy replied he had not learnt of Tlatelolco until recently but found the role of snipers shared between ''Andor'' and the actual event particularly interesting.<ref name="LA Times Espanol" /> [[Eugene Lin]], who played an Imperial security trooper in the Ghorman Massacre, shared a comment on social media likening it to "space Tiananmen Square massacre."<ref name="TianamenSquare">{{Instagram|username=andor.imperial.officer|name=[[Eugene Lin]]|archivefile=File:EugeneLin-TianamenSquadStory-IG.png|nolive=1}}</ref>

[[File:DeniseGoughFreePalestine.png|thumb|right|450px|A social media post by [[Denise Gough]] on ''Andor'' and Palestinian solidarity]]
Dedra Meero's actor, [[Denise Gough]], expressed in interviews her belief that artists like herself using their platforms to speak up for the Palestinian people is important to keep up a groundswell of support through democracies like Ireland for the rights of other people.<ref name="Gough Gaza">{{YouTube|video=_omQdodjg_M|text=‘Andor’ Star: I Refuse to Stay Silent on Gaza|channel=@BreakThroughNews|name=BreakThrough News|date=2025-05-31}}</ref> Gough has said she is touched by ''Andor'' fans who met her and Kyle Soller wearing symbols of solidarity with Palestinians and "countless interactions" ending with the words "free Palestine," which she thought was not just "political" but simply human.<ref name="Gough Manchester">{{Instagram|username=denisegough1|url=p/DMnsoI6NGJT|name=[[Denise Gough]]|date=2025-07-27|quote=Today I spent the day with @kyle_soller at a comic con in Manchester. Thanks for having us @manchestercomiccon There were hundreds of fans of all different shows including our own beloved Andor. Lovely people queued up to be kind to us and take pictures and leave little gifts. What was so beautiful was how many of those lovely people were wearing solidarity pins, badges and keffiyehs. Many times I was thanked for wearing my own. Countless interactions ended with the words ‘free Palestine’. So… in this space where we are told not to be ‘political’ it turns out that everyone really wants to be human. I left feeling hopeful and uplifted that indeed the tide is turning. People are speaking up and helping each other to do the same. We’ve had enough. Free Palestine ❤️💚🖤🤍|archivefile=File:DeniseGoughFreePalestine.png}}</ref> Having visited Palestine in September and witnessed Israel's ongoing occupation of the [[Wikipedia:West Bank|West Bank]] first-hand, Gough said in an interview with journalist [[Wikipedia:Mehdi Hasan|Mehdi Hasan]] that ''Andor''{{'s}} portrayal of the banality of evil particularly resonates with the apparent resurgence of fascism in the world, and she could not see how Israel can be considered a democracy.<ref name="Gough Zeteo">{{WebCite|url=http://zeteo.com/p/exclusive-israel-is-gaslighting-us|text=EXCLUSIVE: Israel is ‘Gaslighting’ Us, Says ‘Andor’ Star After Trip to Occupied West Bank|date=2025-09-22|work=Zeteo|archivedate=20250923232942}}</ref>

Nemik's actor, [[Alex Lawther]], likewise joined the [[Wikipedia:Gaza war protests in the United Kingdom|Palestine solidarity protests]] in London expressing a "collective outrage" and calling on the UK government to take action on the killing of Palestinians,<ref name="Lawther Palestine">{{Facebook|username=palestinesolidarityuk|url=watch/?v=1204726891457112|name=[[Wikipedia:Palestine Solidarity Campaign|Palestine Solidarity Campaign UK]]|date=2025-07-29|archivedate=20250928225213}}</ref> and both Gough and Lawther were among the artists asserting that Israel has been committing genocide in a video projected by the [[Wikipedia:Palestine Solidarity Campaign|Palestine Solidarity Campaign UK]] at the [[Wikipedia:Labour Party Conference|Labour Party Conference]] on [[September 28]], 2025, requesting the UK government to stop arms sales to Israel and to sanction the country.<ref name="Labour conference">{{Instagram|username=palestinesolidarityuk|url=p/DPGP5Sfk76-|name=[[Wikipedia:Palestine Solidarity Campaign|Palestine Solidarity Campaign UK]]|date=2025-09-28|quote=🚨Breaking: Major artists have called on Keir Starmer to accept Israel is committing a genocide in Gaza, in a video projected in Liverpool at the entrance to the Labour party conference site. "The refusal of his government to use the word genocide is a craven way of evading Britain’s legal obligations under the Genocide Convention" -<br />@khalid3bdalla|archiveurl=https://ghostarchive.org/archive/AldN0}}</ref> Stellan Skarsgård believed that bringing one's own experiences into understanding ''Andor'' is the way to treat any series, even though it was written before Trump's re-election and "the horrors of Gaza" that he was worried would happen upon hearing Israeli politicians speaking of "flattening Gaza" and ethnic cleansing right after the October 7 terror attack, and a series like ''Andor'' cannot be written again because "you can be sure that it'll fit the world."<ref name="Stellan Vulture">{{WebCite|url=https://www.vulture.com/article/stellan-skarsgard-in-conversation.html|text=Stellan Skarsgård on Being a Nepo Daddy, ‘Sentimental Value’|author=E. Alex Jung|date=2025-10-22|work=[[Wikipedia:Vulture (website)|''Vulture'']]|archivedate=20251022161555}}</ref> When asked about including the word "genocide" in a ''Star Wars'' series at the ATX TV Festival of [[May 29]]&ndash;[[June 1]], Gilroy expressed that it is sad that many people could find an immediate emotional resonance with the word; Beau Willimon, who wrote the episodes "[[Ever Been to Ghorman?]]" "[[I Have Friends Everywhere]]," and "[[What a Festive Evening]]," mentioned he also found that people could find the series motivating for their own actions, with Gilroy adding to the cheers of the audience that Willimon himself has made personal sacrifices as president of the [[Wikipedia:Writers Guild of America, East|Writers Guild of America, East]].<ref name="ATX Andor" />

[[File:WillimonUkraine2022.jpg|thumb|left|350px|Beau Willimon (right) with President Zelenskyy and Pyotr Verzilov in Ukraine in 2022. Willimon was among the writers of ''Andor'', who spoke about Ukraine just as they spoke about world history.]]
Aside from the labor union, Willimon was also working on a documentary in Ukraine with [[Wikipedia:Noaz Deshe|Noaz Deshe]] and [[Wikipedia:Pyotr Verzilov|Pyotr Verzilov]] on the Russian invasion in spring 2022 during the development of ''Andor'' Season 2,<ref name="Willimon Ukraine" /> including at the site of the [[Wikipedia:Bucha massacre|Bucha massacre]] which Ukrainian authorities have described as genocide.<ref name="Bucha massacre">{{WebCite|url=https://www.fox4news.com/news/house-of-cards-showrunner-is-filming-in-bucha-to-document-genocide-and-heroic-resistance-in-ukraine|text='House of Cards' showrunner is filming in Bucha to document 'genocide' and 'heroic resistance' in Ukraine|author=Tyler O'Neil|date=2022-04-15|work=[[Wikipedia:KDFW|Fox 4 KDFW]]|archivedate=20220415150928}}</ref> The series' writers spoke plenty about Ukraine as they did history with podcasts like [[Wikipedia:Dan Carlin|Dan Carlin's]] [[Wikipedia:Hardcore History|''Hardcore History'']],<ref name="Willimon Ukraine" /> and Willimon said that his experiences in Ukraine certainly informed his work on Ghorman. Cassian Andor and Bix Caleen's domestic life was shown in "Ever Been to Ghorman?" as they tried to make a home of their [[Axis network safehouse|apartment]] and [[Samo's|shop for groceries]] on Coruscant, but all under the consequences of authoritarian rule given Bix Caleen's nightmares and "[[Wikipedia:Post-traumatic stress disorder|PTSD]]," as well as Cassian Andor talking about having needed to kill someone who had seen his partner and the underlying tension of constantly looking behind one's shoulder.<ref name="Willimon 4-6" />

Emmy-nominated costume designer [[Michael Wilkinson]] considered the dramatic tragedy that the Ghor, a "strong community with a strong sense of traditions," would serve in ''Andor'' and made a conscious decision to portray the Ghor as "everything great about humans." To that end, Ghorman fashion was made to be "very traditional and sober and elegant" with inspiration drawn particularly from 1940s and 1950s [[Wikipedia:French fashion#Paris|Paris]] and [[Wikipedia:Italian fashion|Italy]], and this culture becomes a part of Syril Karn's journey in the season as he evolves from the "officecore" look of Coruscant bureaucracy.<ref name="Ankler" />

Dan Gilroy spoke about Mon Mothma's speech on the Ghorman genocide in an interview published on August 19, 2025. Delivered by Genevieve O'Reilly, the speech was inspired by the speeches of US President Abraham Lincoln, UK Prime Minister [[Wikipedia:Winston Churchill|Winston Churchill]], American civil rights leader [[Wikipedia:Martin Luther King Jr.|Martin Luther King Jr.]], and Indian independence activist [[Wikipedia:Mahatma Gandhi|Mahatma Gandhi]] for their sense of gravitas and wisdom. Gilroy added that Mothma raising the issue of "the death of truth" in the Senate was an attempt to bring people out of the "mass hypnosis" created by [[Wikipedia:Big lie|ever bigger lies]] under authoritarian regimes, akin to the methods of Nazi propagandist [[Wikipedia:Joseph Goebbels|Joseph Goebbels]]&mdash;the speech would be like "accusing Hitler of genocide in the Reichstag," the German parliament, and Mothma knew the Empire would seek to execute her for using the word "genocide" because it is "radioactive." Gilroy, whose father was among the soldiers who liberated the [[Wikipedia:Ohrdruf concentration camp|Ohrdruf concentration camp]] in Germany in 1945, added that the term remains contentious to the present day as authoritarian regimes "will vehemently and violently combat anyone trying to say what they’re doing is anything other than righteous."<ref name="Ghorman speech annotated">{{WebCite|url=https://www.latimes.com/entertainment-arts/awards/story/2025-08-19/andor-disney-mon-mothma-senate-speech-annotated-dan-gilroy|text=Mon Mothma's 'Andor' Senate speech, annotated by Dan Gilroy|author=Matt Brennan|date=2025-08-19|work=[[Wikipedia:Los Angeles Times|''Los Angeles Times'']]|archivedate=20250822221122}}</ref> Speaking on May 30, Genevieve O'Reilly had said that the woman she plays must use her voice as her weapon or a powerful tool, and that it is important for art to "give voice to things that we find difficult."<ref name="LA Times Espanol" />

=====The applicability of ''Andor''=====
{{Quote|My overwhelming feeling over the past three months and going out to sell the show is really&mdash;it's sadness. ''[&hellip;]'' We went around the world selling the show, and every place we went&hellip;because I really didn't want to&mdash;particularly in the beginning, I didn't want to have the show get ghettoized by being left or right or political or whatever, I was really, we were really surfing through&hellip;but every journalist, every interview that we did, everywhere we went, people were finding ways to take things&mdash;the same incident and make it applicable for them. 'Oh, this is Gaza, this is Ukraine, this is Northern Ireland, this is&mdash;this is ''me'', man. This is me! This is us!' And that's&mdash;it's very sad. I do worry, as I said before, I do worry that we're into a different situation with tech. I wonder if our fallacy is that we've been going along, going 'oh, here's all these revolutions and we know everything about a revolution and Mike's ''[Mike Duncan]'' done all these shows about it and Tony's written all this stuff and it's all codified and you just pick one from column A, from column B'&mdash;I wonder if there isn't another page in the book that we haven't seen, and that's the page we're on now, and that's the one that worries me the most.|Tony Gilroy, in July 2025, reflecting on ''Andor'', humanity, and the advance of AI|<ref name="Stewart Duncan">{{YouTube|video=SUUw-UfK64Q|text=Andor, Trump and Our Revolutionary Times &#124; The Weekly Show with Jon Stewart|channel=@WeeklyShowPodcast|name=The Weekly Show with Jon Stewart|date=2025-07-10}}</ref>}}
[[File:AndorZinesTracieChing.jpg|thumb|left|350px|Copies of ''[[REBELIÓN: An Andor Zine]]'' on store shelves.]]
O'Reilly also said that "when story is at its best, it's holding a mirror up to us. Perhaps we can see ourselves and our pain and our troubles reflected in something&mdash;and maybe we can ask ourselves some questions." She added that she hoped ''Andor'' can spark more conversations between people, and there is hope if individuals can find their fellow people and connect with each other.<ref name="LA Times Espanol" /> In [[Wikipedia:Ross Douthat|Ross Douthat's]] interview of Gilroy for ''The New York Times'' that was published on [[June 5]], the two discussed how ''Andor'' focused on the destruction of community<ref name="Gilroy Douthat">{{WebCite|url=https://www.nytimes.com/2025/06/05/opinion/film-hollywood-andor-politics.html|text=What Makes Art ‘Left Wing’?|author=[[Wikipedia:Ross Douthat|Ross Douthat]]|date=2025-06-05|work=[[Wikipedia:The New York Times|''The New York Times'']]|archivedate=20250606092427}}</ref> as Gilroy pushed back on Douthat's attempt to frame ''Andor'' along left-leaning political lines; to Gilroy, there is a moralist aspect to ''Andor'' but his characters do not have "left" or "right" demands.<ref name="Reason Gilroy" />

Gilroy also spoke about the future of AI, having discussed AI with people in [[Wikipedia:Los Angeles|Los Angeles]] in the past few days and came across the "terrifying" prospect of a [[Wikipedia:AI alignment|misaligned]] AI superintelligence killing all humans arriving as early as [[2027]], or alternative dystopic scenarios such as mass unemployment and an AI nuclear race between the United States and China.<ref name="Gilroy Douthat" /> In a podcast recorded with the host [[Wikipedia:Jon Stewart|Jon Stewart]] and historian [[Wikipedia:Mike Duncan (podcaster)|Mike Duncan]] on [[July 9]], Gilroy discussed his use of historical revolutions as a catalog for fictional stories, emphasizing the behavioral aspect of individuals&mdash;such as what they fear or the banal selfishness of agents of the state&mdash;and integrating them with the journey of a character like Cassian Andor waking up to political consciousness. Gilroy said he had worked on ideals for the future after the revolution for ''Andor'' but deliberately left it out; overcoming a common obstacle like the Galactic Empire or the Russian tsarist regime took precedent, ahead of any discussion on a desired future after the fight and without a common goal.<ref name="Stewart Duncan" />

Duncan agreed and added that a common feature of revolutions is that they start with ideals and slogans such as freedom and democracy but spiral out of any individual's control, and once they had successfully removed a regime, authority, sovereignty, and legitimacy had to be [[Wikipedia:Constructivism (international relations)|reconstructed]] and inevitably lead to the movement's splintering, allowing the influence of power-seeking figures such as Lenin to dominate. In the same vein, Gilroy said he had adapted the "authoritarian playbook" of "building the enemy you want," giving the examples of [[Wikipedia:J. Edgar Hoover|J. Edgar Hoover]] with the civil rights and anti-war movements, the British state with the [[Wikipedia:Provisional Irish Republican Army|IRA]], or [[Wikipedia:Benjamin Netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] with Hamas, complementing the spontaneous nature of uprisings, such as the [[Wikipedia:Tunisian revolution|martyr]] of the Arab Spring. The podcasters then discussed the United States seemingly becoming a "tinderbox."<ref name="Stewart Duncan" /> 

[[File:Welcome-SenatorialArrest.png|thumb|right|350px|The arrest of Senator Dasi Oran in an episode released in May. Tony Gilroy remarked that ''Andor''{{'s}} creators were shocked at real life Senator Alex Padilla being handcuffed and dragged away from a US federal press conference in June.]]
Gilroy had thought about some of the audiences' remarks that ''Andor'' has been "predictive" of America in the past six months, with the mineral rights over Ghorman echoing the US' ambitions over [[Wikipedia:Greenland|Greenland]] and Ukraine's resources, the migrantion issue on Mina-Rau echoing the US [[Wikipedia:United States Immigration and Customs Enforcement|Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE)]] agency's [[Wikipedia:Deportation in the second presidency of Donald Trump|hardline deportation operations]], and the arrest of Senator Dasi Oran in the Senate Building echoing US Senator [[Wikipedia:Alex Padilla|Alex Padilla]] being forcibly detained at a federal press conference. Gilroy added he was surprised a confrontation over an ICE raid had not yet led to an event akin to the [[Wikipedia:Kent State shootings|Kent State shootings]] during the anti-war protests in 1970 and prompted a declaration of martial law, and he suggested a civil conflict would then perhaps be a desired outcome for the "tech right" like [[Wikipedia:Peter Thiel|Peter Thiel]] of [[Wikipedia:Palantir Technologies|Palantir Technologies]] who are using Trump as a "host organism" and, as expressed in Thiel's interview with Ross Douthat on June 26, would not care if AI wipes out humanity.<ref name="Stewart Duncan" />

Gilroy recalled that the fantasy author [[Wikipedia:J. R. R. Tolkien|J. R. R. Tolkien]] disliked allegories and pointed out that they are different from applicability&mdash;Gilroy realized that the audience of ''Andor'' have found the story applicable to many different contexts. Although he was hesitant, especially at the start of Season 2's press tour, to let interviewers associate political labels with ''Andor'', he has felt sadness as people around the world find ''Andor'' resonating deeply with their own experiences. Gilroy's greatest concern was that although ''Andor'' and Mike Duncan's podcasts have examined patterns of human history, humanity may be compromising ourselves with the development of advanced AI. The podcast speakers nevertheless expressed hope that the series, having become part of contemporary popular culture with phrases like "I have friends everywhere" appearing at anti-authoritarian protests such as America's [[Wikipedia:June 2025 No Kings protests|No Kings Day]] demonstrations on [[June 14]], could help people adapt to the changing times.<ref name="Stewart Duncan" /> Indeed, the sign-up webpage of the American [[Wikipedia:Indivisible movement|Indivisible movement]], titled "Join Our Fight to Restore the Republic," uses a photograph of a placard bearing the words "I have friends everywhere" by the flag of the USA.<ref name="Indivisible">{{WebCite|url=https://act.indivisible.org/signup/friendseverywhere|text=Join Our Fight to Restore the Republic|work=[[Wikipedia:Indivisible movement|Indivisible]]|archivedate=20250911194035}}</ref>

[[File:IHaveFriendsEverywhereProtest.jpg|thumb|left|350px|Protests against Trump's presidency in the United States have adopted the phrase "I have friends everywhere" from ''Andor''.]]
''Star Wars'' imagery had already been invoked in pro-democracy protests in Hong Kong<ref name="Journal" /> and Thailand during the youth-led pan-Asian [[Wikipedia:Milk Tea Alliance|Milk Tea Alliance]] movement in 2020,<ref name="Thai rubber ducks" /> as well as during the [[Wikipedia:2024 South Korean martial law crisis|2024 protests]] in [[Wikipedia:South Korea|South Korea]]<ref name="Korea martial law">{{Twitter|username=tokkicomrade|url=status/1872889732189044825|name=블락톡기|archiveurl=https://web.archive.org/web/20241230084502/https://www.reddit.com/r/StarWarsCantina/comments/1ho6pfo/an_amazing_star_wars_flag_in_the_south_korean}}</ref> and the [[Wikipedia:2025 Indonesian protests|March 2025 protests]] in [[Wikipedia:Indonesia|Indonesia]].<ref name="Indonesia armed forces">{{Getty|subdomain=www|url=detail/news-photo/students-protest-against-a-revision-to-the-armed-forces-law-news-photo/2205497981|text=Student Protest Against Military Law|author=NurPhoto|date=2025-03-20|lang=co.uk}}</ref> Nemik's line that "freedom is a pure idea" from ''Andor'' Season 1 has been used in the 2025 Palestine solidarity protests in London.<ref name="Palestine London">{{Twitter|username=sw_holocron|url=status/1969840587097682419|name=Star Wars Holocron|archiveurl=https://ghostarchive.org/archive/MVvqJ}}</ref> Placards inspired by ''Andor'' Season 2 have also been used in the [[Wikipedia:September 2025 Philippine protests|September 2025 protests]] in the [[Wikipedia:Philippines|Philippines]] as part of the [[Wikipedia:Asian Spring|Asian Spring]].<ref name="Philippines corruption">{{Facebook|username=groups/1437911546518511|url=groups/Star.Wars.Philippines/posts/3815844325391876|name=A contributor to Star Wars Philippines|date=2025-09-21|quote=Spotted this in today's rally at Luneta|archivedate=20250928221619}}</ref>

In an interview with [[Wikipedia:Charlie Sykes|Charlie Sykes]] released on [[August 12]], 2025, Tony Gilroy said that as someone brought up amid a strong belief in American democracy following World War II<ref name="Charlie Sykes Gilroy" />&mdash;Dan Gilroy had said in another interview that their father taught them that crimes against humanity like the Ohrdruf concentration camp he had helped liberate in 1945 should not be forgotten, whitewashed, or ignored<ref name="Ghorman speech annotated" />&mdash;it has been shocking how history has developed in his own lifetime.
