While ''Andor'' was written as a non-ideological adventure focused on characters, Gilroy mentioned that ''Andor''{{'s}} crew was wondering in 2024 how the US election results would impact the series' reception upon its release in 2025; the writer thought that Trump's second presidency has been adding an extra dimension to it, and that "at this point," he is comfortable with fans comparing ''Andor''{{'s}} use of the word "genocide" with using that word in real life in relation with Israel's actions in Gaza.<ref name="Charlie Sykes Gilroy" />

Gilroy had also heard that the singing of "We Are the Ghor" in the fictional [[Ghor (language)|Ghor]] language sounded like various real life languages such as [[Wikipedia:Ukrainian language|Ukrainian]] to different people, and while the "horror show" of history repeats and makes ''Andor'' immediately resonant, he has become gravely concerned that a "tech [[Wikipedia:Reich|Reich]]," whether it be Palantir or [[Wikipedia:Xi Jinping|Xi Jinping]], is building AI that would be more dangerous and intractable than anything in history, and there is no leader who could make a rational and global plan to control this development.<ref name="Charlie Sykes Gilroy" /> Tony Gilroy also said in an interview with Pop Culture Confidential released on [[August 19]] that as a post&ndash;World War II child who lived in America from the civil rights movement and Vietnam War to the COVID-19 pandemic who "thought I had lived through all the history I was going to get," he viewed history as progressive, with struggles despite being bloody ultimately resulting in better overall outcomes, until very recently&mdash;"you want to live after antibiotics and before the robots take over."<ref name="PCC Gilroy">{{YouTube|video=4rjat6fyv5w|text=A Conversation with Tony Gilroy — Andor, Revolution, and the Forces & Choices That Drive Us|channel=@PopCultureConfidential|name=Pop Culture Confidential|date=2025-08-19}}</ref> 

[[File:TonyGilroy-SWcom.jpg|thumb|right|250px|Tony Gilroy, the creator and showrunner of ''Andor'']]
In an interview with Backstory Magazine released on [[August 24]], Tony Gilroy and Dan Gilroy were asked about how ''Andor'' resonates with contemporary politics. Tony said that both ''Andor'' and modern states borrow from the same "fascist playbook," or rather the "authoritarian playbook" since "fascism is used as a universal term for authoritarianism," that has existed for thousands of years of human history and been documented since at least the time of the Roman historian [[Wikipedia:Suetonius|Suetonius]]. Dan added that "Who Are You?" begins and ends with HoloNet News propaganda due to his interest in the role of mass media since making the 2014 film [[Wikipedia:Nightcrawler (film)|''Nightcrawler'']], believing that the United States lost "any credible news the second that the news rooms had to make money" in his lifetime because news became entertainment that has been stripped of context. As for Mon Mothma's speech in the next episode, the key sentiment for Dan as the writer was its meaning to those who may be frustrated that the longstanding democratic bodies they have cherished in the real world are being undermined.<ref name="Gilroys Backstory" /> Dan told [[Wikipedia:Empire (magazine)|''Empire Magazine'']] for its December 2025 issue that he wrote the speech in 2023 with anger towards American members of Congress "abandoning their democratic principles and bending their knee to power."<ref name="Dan Empire speech">{{WebCite|url=https://www.empireonline.com/tv/news/andor-mon-mothma-senate-speech-hallowed-ground-exclusive|text=Andor Mon Mothma Speech &#x27;Was Hallowed Ground&#x27; For Dan Gilroy|author=Ben Travis|date=2025-12-15|work=[[Wikipedia:Empire (magazine)|''Empire Magazine'']]|archivedate=20251216070404}}</ref>

=====The politics of narrative art=====
{{Quote|We've given people a narrative to relate to politics in a more story-based way.|Dan Gilroy|<ref name="Gilroy Emmy press">{{YouTube|video=a5g0YvOQ-HI|text=EMMYS 2025- Dan Gilroy (ANDOR), winner Outstanding Writing for a Drama Series|channel=@cherrythegeektv8053|name=Cherry the Geek TV|date=2025-09-15}}</ref>}}
In an interview with [[Wikipedia:The Bulwark (website)|''The Bulwark'']] released on [[September 10]], Tony Gilroy remarked that the cast and crew of ''Andor'' had no orders from Disney and were initially tight-lipped when being interviewed about real world politics so as to maintain an audience with a diverse spread of opinions, but the creators gradually loosened up with their own individual opinions. By the end of the publicity period, the creators have felt that it was no longer their responsibility to refrain from linking the series to politics because politics has gone beyond ideological left-right divisions. Instead, they morbidly joked that Trump official [[Wikipedia:Stephen Miller (advisor)|Stephen Miller]] was following the series and believed the present governments of the United States, Israel, and Russia are "matching the show"&mdash;"it's fascism, man." Gilroy highlighted Trump's deployment of the [[Wikipedia:National Guard (United States)|National Guard]] on the streets of [[Wikipedia:Washington, D.C.|Washington, D.C.]] and the president posting an ''Apocalypse Now'' meme about the "[[Wikipedia:United States Department of Defense|Department of War]]" napalm-bombing Chicago and the "nationalization" of companies like [[Wikipedia:Intel|Intel]] by open bribery, which he thought contrasts starkly with the "passivity" of the people.<ref name="Bulwark">{{YouTube|video=iaqnHfa6n4E|text=Tony Gilroy: MAGA’s Moronic Fake Patriotism|channel=@PopCultureConfidential|name=Pop Culture Confidential|date=2025-08-19}}</ref>

As such, Tony Gilroy felt that perhaps Nemik's point in Season 1&mdash;that the "pace of oppression outstrips our ability to understand it"&mdash;has come true, all the while making America "a second-rate power" when compared to [[Wikipedia:2025 China Victory Day Parade|China's victory parade in Tiananmen Square]] with Russia, North Korea, and other authoritarian regimes. Gilroy asserted that ''Andor'' does not advocate leftism or right-wing or progressive ideology or has any relation to the US' political parties&mdash;''Andor''{{'s}} ideas are moral and center around the notion of the destruction of communities, and he thought that he would not know how to write such things in fiction as reality has become so surreal. He concluded that people had forgotten that their lives are owed to someone's sacrifice in the past and how good it feels to be courageous, to make sacrifices, and to "stand up for the things you ''know'' are absolutely right."<ref name="Bulwark" />

Dan Gilroy won an Emmy Award for his writing on ''Andor'' on [[September 14]]. Gilroy credited the fans for engaging with the series beyond just watching it, mentioned that he shared many fans' sentiment that it has ongoing real life parallels, and thanked Bob Iger for backing ''Andor'' as a story about "ordinary people who are caught in extraordinary circumstances." Gilroy also thought that ''Andor''{{'s}} creators have given audiences "a narrative to relate to politics in a more story-based way," and he as a writer was motivated by his belief that "there are things worth dying for."<ref name="Gilroy Emmy press" /> On [[September 19]], Dan Gilroy wrote in [[Wikipedia:Deadline Hollywood|''Deadline'']] magazine's opinion column that the Disney corporation faces a choice of either paving way for what he characterized as the most venomous "evil" in America's history or defending the [[Wikipedia:First Amendment to the United States Constitution|US Constitution's First Amendment]] following Disney's [[Wikipedia:Suspension of Jimmy Kimmel Live!|suspension]] of the [[Wikipedia:Jimmy Kimmel Live!|''Jimmy Kimmel Live!'']] talk show due to government pressure, which Gilroy compared to Putin's silencing of shows that criticized him upon taking power in post-Soviet Russia. [[Wikipedia:Jimmy Kimmel|Jimmy Kimmel's]] suspension prompted a protest at [[Wikipedia:Walt Disney Studios (Burbank)|Disney's headquarters]] by writers' and actors' guilds with signs, including some which read "Did you even watch ''Andor''?" and "This is literally what your show ''Andor'' is about."<ref name="Dan Gilroy Kimmel">{{WebCite|url=https://deadline.com/2025/09/andor-dan-gilroy-jimmy-kimmel-commentary-1236549627|text=‘Andor’ Writer Dan Gilroy On Disney Suspending Jimmy Kimmel & Hollywood Facing “Venomous Evil”: “This Isn’t A Skirmish. It’s A Siege” – Guest Column|author=[[Dan Gilroy]]|date=2025-09-19|work=[[Wikipedia:Deadline Hollywood|''Deadline'']]|archivedate=20250920000606}}</ref>

Disney reinstated Kimmel [[September 22|three days later]], with the [[Wikipedia:Governor of Illinois|Governor of Illinois]] quoting a line about oppression by Nemik in ''Andor''.<ref name="Kimmel reinstated">{{Twitter|username=GovPritzker|url=status/1970222348516118599|name=Governor JB Pritzker|date=2025-09-22|quote="Tyranny requires constant effort. It breaks, it leaks. Authority is brittle. Oppression is the mask of fear. Remember that."<br /><br /><br />Looking forward to seeing Jimmy back on the air.|archiveurl=https://ghostarchive.org/archive/E7pB8}}</ref> Tony Gilroy had said on April 15 that power has always wanted to control the narrative since the first campfire story, citing the Reichstag fire as the Nazi pretext for persecuting communists and Jews, the [[Wikipedia:Gulf of Tonkin incident|Gulf of Tonkin incident]] as the American pretext for expanding the war in Vietnam, and [[Wikipedia:William Randolph Hearst|William Randolph Hearst's]] press campaign that pressured America to wage [[Wikipedia:Spanish–American War|war]] against the Spanish Empire. Aside from history, the ''Andor'' showrunner was also inspired by the series' actors as he was writing and greatly appreciated that the crew was able "to build a community that's vaguely utopian while making a show about the destruction of community&mdash;that was powerful."<ref name="Gilroy April 15" />

George Lucas' alma mater, the [[Wikipedia:University of Southern California|University of Southern California's]] [[Wikipedia:USC School of Cinematic Arts|School of Cinematic Arts]], organized "War, Justice, and Democracy in ''ANDOR: A STAR WARS STORY''," two days of ''Andor'' screenings and conversations with Tony and Dan Gilroy on [[October 12]] and [[October 13|13]], in partnership with Disney+. They were hosted by the [[Wikipedia:International Criminal Court|International Criminal Court's]] founding chief prosecutor [[Wikipedia:Luis Moreno Ocampo|Luis Moreno Ocampo]] and filmmaker Professor [[Wikipedia:Ted Braun|Ted Braun]].<ref name="USC Andor">{{WebCite|url=https://visionsandvoices.usc.edu/events/50657585073395|text=War, Justice, and Democracy in ANDOR: A STAR WARS STORY – Part I|work=[[Wikipedia:University of Southern California|University of Southern California]]|archivedate=20251017185730}} and {{WebCite|url=https://visionsandvoices.usc.edu/events/50829124887446|text=War, Justice, and Democracy in ANDOR: A STAR WARS STORY – Part II|work=[[Wikipedia:University of Southern California|University of Southern California]]|archivedate=20251020040128}}</ref> Following the event, Tony Gilroy recorded a video urging fellow Americans to join the [[Wikipedia:October 2025 No Kings protests|No Kings protests]] on [[October 18]]&mdash;"democracy is up for grabs and it's up to us to protect the thing we love." ''Andor'' actors Diego Luna, Denise Gough, and [[Benjamin Norris]] (who played [[Bardi]]) jointly posted the video with the Indivisible campaign group.<ref name="No Kings October">{{Instagram|username=indivisibleteam|url=reel/DP370AOEcWy|name=[[Wikipedia:Indivisible movement|Indivisible]]|date=2025-10-16|quote=We have friends everywhere — nearly 3,000 peaceful protests are planned across the country on Saturday. Join your local No Kings event to make it clear we won’t bend the knee to an authoritarian power grab. #nokings|archivefile=File:GilroyNoKings-Instagram.png}}</ref> Din Djarin actor [[Pedro Pascal]] met Tony Gilroy on the day of what Pascal characterizes as pro-democracy protests in Los Angeles,<ref name="Pascal No Kings">{{Instagram|username=pascalispunk|url=p/DP-NtvuCQPC|name=[[Pedro Pascal]]|date=2025-10-18|quote=#NoKings #LosAngeles PROTECT #Democracy|archivefile=File:PedroPascal-NoKingsWithGilroy-Instagram.png}}</ref> where [[Wikipedia:The Guardian|''The Guardian'']] found Gilroy, who reiterated his alarm about his work on authoritarianism and the courage needed for resisting it in ''Star Wars'' materializing in contemporary America and expressed dismay at people still lacking an organized leadership while faced with the "karaoke fascist playbook."<ref name="No Kings October Guardian">{{WebCite|url=https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/live/2025/oct/18/anti-trump-no-kings-protests-updates|text=No Kings protest: millions march against Trump in nationwide day of protest – as it happened|author=Lauren Gambino|date=2025-10-18|work=[[Wikipedia:The Guardian|''The Guardian'']]|archivedate=20251019154230}}</ref> [[Wikipedia:New York City Comptroller|New York City comptroller]] and politician [[Wikipedia:Brad Lander|Brad Lander]] posted photos of the No Kings protests with the line "I have friends everywhere."<ref name="Lander No Kings">{{Twitter|username=bradlander|url=status/1979597568280846623|name=[[Wikipedia:Brad Lander|Brad Lander]]|date=2025-10-18|quote=I have friends everywhere.|archiveurl=https://archive.ph/9n9ge}} &mdash; quote repost of a video of the protest in Times Square, NYC by Oliya Scootercaster</ref>

Tony Gilroy spoke with [[Wikipedia:Nick Gillespie|Nick Gillespie]] on the American libertarian [[Wikipedia:Reason Foundation|''Reason'']] podcast in December 2025. Gilroy reiterated his points on ''Andor'' being about ordinary people forced to make choices under authoritarianism and how, with conversations about left-right political ideologies with interviewers such as Ross Douthat for ''The New York Times'' in June, his understanding of the series came to center on "the destruction of community" as the main theme, and "decency and compassion" are really worth fighting for in the real world; audiences draw parallels to concurrent world politics because a basic "personal decency aspect" is clearly being destroyed. Gillespie asked about the "authoritarian surveillance bureaucracy" shown in ''Andor'' being "more terrifying" than the brute force of the Death Star, to which Gilroy answered that anything that feels real to the audience would have more of an emotional impact, especially with the psychological motivations and behavior of characters who worry about pedantic things and willfully forget their choices, engaging with and following commands in bureaucracies that support authoritarianism, such as the Empire's nationalization of the Preox-Morlana corporation leading to the destruction of the Ferrix community, the infrastructure projects on Aldhani made with the colonial displacement and suppression of its people, the audit of Mina-Rau leading to the crackdown on the migrant workers, and the systematic dismantling of the substantial political, economic, and cultural community of Ghorman with complex logistics such as galaxy-wide propaganda through state-owned media down to the personal tragedies of the bureaucrats Syril Karn&mdash;a romantic fantasist who is lonely and seeks belonging, akin to the "loneliness epidemic" afflicting people in relatively very prosperous and free societies like America&mdash;and Dedra Meero&mdash;raised as a child robbed of her parents under the yoke of a state-run "kinderblock" in an authoritarian society.<ref name="Reason Gilroy" />

=====Streets of Minneapolis=====
[[File:Minnesota2026.png|thumb|right|350px|Protesters in Minnesota holding up "rebel loon" placards, which read "We are Minnesota. We protect our neighbors."]]
In January 2026, US Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE) [[Wikipedia:Operation Metro Surge|shot dead]] two Americans on the streets of Minneapolis. ''Star Wars'' fans on social media circulated a petition for Lucasfilm to divest from convention organizer ReedPop for 2027's [[Celebration Los Angeles]], given that ReedPop's parent company is [[Wikipedia:RELX|RELX]], which also owns [[Wikipedia:LexisNexis|LexisNexis]], a provider of personal data to ICE. ReedPop subsequently issued a social media statement clarifying that they "do not sell customer information to" either the [[Wikipedia:United States Department of Homeland Security|US Department of Homeland Security]] or ICE, and that ReedPop's data is not shared for commercial use by another company under RELX.<ref name="ReedPop">{{WebCite|url=https://www.comicsbeat.com/reedpop-ice-statement|text=ReedPop responds to questions over relationship with ICE|author=Christopher Chiu-Tabet|date=2026-01-29|work=''The Beat''|archivedate=20260131162853}}</ref> Tattoo artists in Minneapolis and neighboring [[Wikipedia:Saint Paul, Minnesota|Saint Paul]] of [[Wikipedia:Minnesota|Minnesota]] shared a "rebel [[Wikipedia:Loon|loon]]" design, incorporating Minnesota's state bird with the Rebel Alliance starbird as a symbol of community amidst growing authoritarianism. The rebel loon was among other tattoos given as part of a fundraiser for a local immigrant rights group.<ref name="Rebel loon">{{WebCite|url=https://www.cbsnews.com/minnesota/news/rebel-loon-design-tattoo|text=Rebel loon design quickly becoming popular request at Twin Cities tattoo parlors|author=Nick Lunemann|date=2026-01-27|work=[[Wikipedia:CBS News|CBS News]]|archivedate=20260131164247}}</ref>

Tony Gilroy reiterated his points on fascism and narrative in an interview with ''The Hollywood Reporter'' published on [[February 20]], 2026, when asked about events in America. Gilroy observed that narrative art like ''Star Wars'' use historical models to accomodate the cast and audience's broad political spectrum, and he himself preferred sensible problem-solving from the center, center-left, and center-right but lamented their political leaders' failure of narrative, of effective communication and messaging. Gilroy opined that his greatest fear of the attacks on democracy is not just America having to contend with its narratively incompetent fascism and strategic losses to China but the "tech Reich" and AI, given that American civil society and leaders in Europe are still struggling to understand and discuss AI's threats to society as we know it.<ref name="Gilroy THR 2026">{{WebCite|url=https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/tv/tv-features/andor-creator-tony-gilroy-gives-the-interview-1236510166|text=‘Andor’ Creator Tony Gilroy Gives the Interview He Couldn’t During Its Release|author=Brian Davids|date=2026-02-20|work=[[Wikipedia:The Hollywood Reporter|The Hollywood Reporter]]|archivedate=20260220200855}}</ref>

George Lucas articulated his answer to "how do you build a society?" in March 2026: "you do not ''build'' a society," as it instead is based on community, family, nationhood, and people being "glued together" by mythology. He defined mythology as the stories that people believe in&mdash;not because they are true but because they are emotional.<ref name="Film and narrative art" />

====The future of ''Star Wars''====
Daisy Ridley would reprise her role as Rey Skywalker in a [[Untitled Star Wars film (Sharmeen Obaid-Chinoy)|''Star Wars'' film]] being developed with [[Sharmeen Obaid-Chinoy]] as the director and [[George Nolfi]] as a screenwriter. In February 2025, Nolfi noted how George Lucas' ''Star Wars'' is "very steeped in broad notions of politics," whereby individuals organize their society against chaos only to have their tools become weapons of oppression, ranging from antiquity to modern times with the Roman Empire and Nazis&mdash;but unlike attending a philosophy or political science class or reading the news of today, ''Star Wars'' makes sense of reality in an emotionally resonant way.<ref name="Nolfi">{{WebCite|url=https://filmstories.co.uk/news/star-wars-george-nolfi-on-his-new-jedi-order-screenplay-exclusive|text=Star Wars #124; George Nolfi on his New Jedi Order screenplay (exclusive)|author=Ryan Lambie|date=2025-02-03|work=[https://filmstories.co.uk Film Stories]|archivedate=20250203212158}}</ref> In the same month, Daisy Ridley similarly expressed the idea that all ''Star Wars'' movies are political and emotionally relatable, with "the individual versus the big corporation or the big group."<ref name="Ridley Inverse">{{WebCite|url=https://www.inverse.com/entertainment/daisy-ridley-cleaner-star-wars-new-jedi-order|text="They're All Political": Daisy Ridley Teases Her New Star Wars Movie|author=Dais Johnson|date=2025-02-11|work=[[Wikipedia:Inverse (website)|''Inverse'']]|archivedate=20250212102426}}</ref>

===LEGO===
In the non-canon 2022 video game ''[[LEGO Star Wars: The Skywalker Saga]]'', Padmé Amidala shouts "For the Republic! For democracy!" upon being selected as a player character. During players' space battle against the ''Home One'', Captain [[Fo Skoolan]] declares that ship is a part of the Galactic Republic and that the player should "prepare to be shot down in the name of democracy."<ref name="TSS">''[[LEGO Star Wars: The Skywalker Saga]]''</ref>
